### 2. APPLICATION TYPE & TECHNOLOGY STACK

**Type:** Full-stack web application - AI Agent Marketplace & Management Portal

**Frontend:**
- **Framework:** Nuxt 4 (Vue 3)
- **UI Library:** @nuxt/ui (UI components)
- **State Management:** Pinia
- **Styling:** Tailwind CSS 4
- **Build Tool:** Vite (via Nuxt)
- **Language:** TypeScript
- **Key Dependencies:**
  - `vue3-google-signin` - Google OAuth integration
  - `@tiptap/vue-3` - Rich text editor
  - `apexcharts`, `chart.js` - Data visualization
  - `html2pdf.js`, `jspdf` - PDF generation
  - `xlsx` - Excel export

**Backend:**
- **Runtime:** Node.js 22 (Alpine)
- **Framework:** Express.js 5
- **Language:** TypeScript (executed via tsx)
- **Database:** PostgreSQL 15+ (no ORM - raw SQL queries)
- **Key Dependencies:**
  - `@google-cloud/*` - GCP integrations (AI Platform, Storage, Secret Manager, Cloud Tasks)
  - `google-auth-library` - OAuth token verification
  - `jsonwebtoken` - JWT handling
  - `swagger-ui-express` - API documentation
  - `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` - MCP (Model Context Protocol) support
  - `multer` - File uploads
  - `pg` - PostgreSQL driver

**Development:**
- **Build:** Multi-stage Docker (Node 22 Alpine)
- **CI/CD:** Google Cloud Build
- **Testing:** Playwright (@playwright/test)
- **Linting:** ESLint 9

---

### 3. DOCKER & CONTAINER CONFIGURATION

**Dockerfile Structure (4 stages):**

| Stage | Purpose | Base Image |
|-------|---------|-----------|
| `dependencies` | Production dependencies only | node:22-alpine |
| `dev-dependencies` | All dependencies (build stage) | node:22-alpine |
| `builder` | Build Nuxt frontend & TypeScript | Uses dev-dependencies |
| `runner` | Production runtime (final image) | node:22-alpine |

**Key Docker Features:**
- **BuildKit optimization** with cache mounts for npm and .nuxt
- **Non-root user** (nodejs:1001) for security
- **Multi-stage build** reduces final image size
- **Security hardening:** Read-only file permissions on dist/ and backend/
- **Health check:** `GET /health` endpoint (30s interval, 40s start delay)
- **Graceful shutdown:** Uses dumb-init for proper signal handling
- **Port:** 8080 (configured via ENV in Dockerfile)
- **Memory limit:** NODE_OPTIONS sets --max-old-space-size=512

**Build Arguments:**
- `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - Frontend OAuth client ID (build-time)
- `NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` - Environment name (production/staging/dev)
- `BUILD_DATE` - Build timestamp
- `VCS_REF` - Git commit SHA
- `COMMIT_SHA` - Short commit hash (written to version_commit.txt)

**Entrypoint:** `/app/docker-entrypoint.sh`
- Validates all required environment variables
- Checks database, GCS, GCP, and auth configurations
- Starts Express API server via `npm run server`

---

### 4. CI/CD PIPELINE (Google Cloud Build)

**File:** `cloudbuild.yaml`

**Pipeline Steps:**

1. **Setup BuildKit Builder** (120s)
   - Creates docker-container driver for advanced caching

2. **Build Docker Image** (1200s = 20 min)
   - Uses multi-stage build with registry-based caching
   - Cache types: remote registry, inline, image-based fallback
   - Tags: commit-SHA, branch-name, latest
   - Platform: linux/amd64
   - Compression: zstd level 3

3. **Push Cache Tag** (30s)
   - Maintains BuildKit cache reference

4. **Deploy to Cloud Run** (300s)
   - Image tag: `${_IMAGE_PATH}:${COMMIT_SHA}`
   - Revision suffix: `${SHORT_SHA}-${BUILD_ID}`
   - Labels applied for tracking

5. **Update Traffic Routing** (180s)
   - Routes 100% traffic to LATEST revision

**Build Configuration:**
- Machine type: E2_HIGHCPU_8 (8 vCPU)
- Disk size: 200GB (for large node_modules)
- Logging: Cloud Logging only
- Substitution variables:
  - `_IMAGE_PATH` - Container registry path
  - `_ENVIRONMENT` - production/staging/dev
  - `_VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - Frontend OAuth ID
  - `_SERVICE_NAME` - Cloud Run service name
  - `_REGION` - GCP region
  - `_PROJECT_ID` - GCP project ID
  - `_CLOUD_RUN_CPU/MEMORY` - Instance resources
  - `_MIN_INSTANCES`, `_MAX_INSTANCES` - Autoscaling
  - `_CONCURRENCY` - Requests per instance
  - `_TIMEOUT` - Request timeout (300s)

**Branch Mapping:**
- `main` → Production environment
- `staging` → Staging environment
- `develop` → Development environment

---

### 5. ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES & CONFIGURATION

**Critical Environment Variables:**

**Server:**
- `PORT` - Server port (default: 8080 in container, 3002 dev)
- `NODE_ENV` - production/development (enforced in production)
- `ENVIRONMENT` - Application environment name
- `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` - CORS origins (comma-separated)

**Database (PostgreSQL):**
- `DB_HOST` - Database hostname (supports Unix socket for Cloud SQL)
- `DB_PORT` - Database port (default: 5432)
- `DB_NAME` - Database name
- `DB_USER` - Database username
- `DB_PASSWORD` - Database password
- `DB_POOL_MIN` - Connection pool minimum (default: 2)
- `DB_POOL_MAX` - Connection pool maximum (default: 10)
- `DATABASE_URL` - Alternative: Full connection string (overrides individual DB_* vars)

**Google Cloud:**
- `GCP_PROJECT_ID` - GCP project for storage & tasks
- `GCP_LOCATION` - GCP region (europe-west4)

**Vertex AI (Google's Generative AI):**
- `VERTEX_AI_PROJECT_ID` - Separate AI project
- `VERTEX_AI_LOCATION` - Region (us-central1)
- `VERTEX_AI_MODEL` - Model name (gemini-2.0-flash-exp)

**RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation):**
- `RAG_PROJECT_ID` - Project for RAG corpus
- `RAG_LOCATION` - Region (europe-west4)
- `RAG_CORPUS_ID` - Corpus ID for document retrieval
- `RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN` - Authentication token
- `RAG_SECRET_NAME` - Secret Manager reference

**Google Cloud Storage:**
- `GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET` - Bucket for image uploads
- `GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET` - Bucket for video uploads
- `GCS_RAG_BUCKET` - Bucket for RAG documents

**File Upload:**
- `MAX_FILE_UPLOAD_SIZE_MB` - Max upload size (default: 100MB)

**Authentication & Security:**
- `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - OAuth client ID for backend verification
- `JWT_SECRET` - REQUIRED in production, encryption key for session tokens
- `JWT_COOKIE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` - Cookie expiration (default: 7 days)
- `JWT_EXPIRY` - Token expiration format (7d)
- `DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN` - Fallback email domain restriction (konecta.com)
- `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - Frontend OAuth client ID (build-time variable)

**Email/Notifications (ChannelHub):**
- `CHANNELHUB_URL` - API endpoint
- `CHANNELHUB_LOGIN_URL` - Auth endpoint
- `CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID` - Client credentials
- `CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` - Client credentials
- `CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID` - Connection identifier

**Cloud Tasks (Async Jobs):**
- `CLOUD_TASKS_PROJECT_ID` - Project for task queue
- `CLOUD_TASKS_QUEUE` - Queue name (rag-ingestion-poc)
- `CLOUD_TASKS_LOCATION` - Region (europe-west1)
- `CLOUD_TASKS_HANDLER_URL` - Webhook endpoint for task completion

**Frontend:**
- `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - OAuth client ID (injected at build time)
- `NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` - Environment (dev/staging/production)
- `NUXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_SPACE_CHAT_URL` - Google Chat space URL

**Logging:**
- `LOG_LEVEL` - info/debug/warn/error

---

### 6. DATABASE & EXTERNAL SERVICE DEPENDENCIES

**Database: PostgreSQL**

**Tables (15+ migrations):**
1. Users - Roles: user, admin, agent_manager, demo_manager, training_manager, usecase_manager, usecase_viewer
2. Agents - AI agents in marketplace
3. Agent Technologies - Many-to-many mapping
4. MCP Servers - Model Context Protocol server configurations
5. Reviews - Agent reviews and ratings
6. Configuration - Application settings
7. Webhook Notifications - Event-based notifications
8. Demos - Live demonstration URLs
9. Chat History - Conversation logs
10. Usage Analytics - Tracking & statistics
11. Solutions - Solution catalog
12. Use Cases - User case management
13. Courses - Training content
14. Success Stories - Case studies
15. Settings - Dynamic configuration (security domains, etc.)

**Connection Modes:**
- Direct TCP: `host:port` for standard databases
- Unix socket: `/cloudsql/instance-connection-name` for Cloud SQL (no port needed)

**External Services:**

| Service | Purpose | SDK |
|---------|---------|-----|
| Google OAuth 2.0 | User authentication | google-auth-library |
| Google Vertex AI | LLM inference (Gemini models) | @google-cloud/vertexai |
| Google AI Platform | Model deployment & management | @google-cloud/aiplatform |
| Google Cloud Storage | File hosting (images, videos, RAG docs) | @google-cloud/storage |
| Google Secret Manager | Secure credential storage | @google-cloud/secret-manager |
| Google Cloud Tasks | Async job queue (RAG ingestion) | @google-cloud/tasks |
| Google Cloud SQL | Managed PostgreSQL database | (via native connection) |
| ChannelHub API | Email/notification service | REST API (custom client) |
| Model Context Protocol | External tool integration | @modelcontextprotocol/sdk |

---

### 7. BUILD & DEPLOYMENT PROCESS

**Local Development Build:**
```bash
# Frontend (Nuxt dev server on port 3000)
npm run dev

# Backend (Express on port 3002)
cd backend && npm run backend
# or from root: npm run server

# Database migrations (auto-run on backend startup)
npm run migrate:status
npm run db:migrate
```

**Production Docker Build:**
```bash
docker build -t koniq-portal:local \
  --build-arg VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=$CLIENT_ID \
  --build-arg COMMIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD) \
  .
```

**Cloud Run Deployment Pipeline:**

1. **Cloud Build** receives code push
2. **BuildKit** builds multi-stage Docker image
3. Image pushed to Google Container Registry/Artifact Registry
4. **Cloud Run** service updated with new image
5. **Traffic routing** automatically switches to new revision
6. Health checks validate 200 status on `/health` endpoint

**Deployment Environments:**
- **dev** (develop branch) → Development environment
- **staging** (staging branch) → Staging environment
- **production** (main branch) → Production environment

**Versioning Strategy:**
- **version.txt** - Semantic version (currently 0.9.1)
- **version_commit.txt** - Git commit SHA (auto-generated during Docker build)
- **Display format:** "0.9.1 (a34f5234)" shown in footer and admin sidebar
- API endpoint: `GET /api/version`

---

### 8. KUBERNETES & INFRASTRUCTURE REFERENCES

**Deployment Target:** Google Cloud Run (serverless containers)

**Infrastructure Components:**

| Component | Type | Details |
|-----------|------|---------|
| Cloud Run Service | Serverless | Managed container runtime |
| Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) | Managed DB | 16 with private IP in VPC |
| VPC Connector | Networking | Connects Cloud Run to database |
| Cloud Storage Buckets | Object Storage | Images, videos, RAG documents |
| Cloud Build | CI/CD | Automated builds on git push |
| Container Registry | Registry | Stores Docker images |
| Secret Manager | Secrets | JWT_SECRET, OAuth credentials |
| Cloud Tasks Queue | Job Queue | RAG ingestion async jobs |

**VPC Setup Required:**
- VPC with custom subnets
- VPC Connector for Cloud Run to Cloud SQL access
- Firewall rules for internal traffic
- VPC Peering for private service access (Cloud SQL)

**Service Accounts:**
- `koniq-portal@${PROJECT_ID}.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
- Roles: Storage Object Admin, AI Platform User, Cloud SQL Client, Secret Manager Secret Accessor

**Cloud Run Configuration:**
- Region: europe-west1
- Min instances: 0 (scales to 0 when unused)
- Max instances: 10
- CPU: 1 vCPU
- Memory: 512 MB
- Timeout: 300 seconds
- Concurrency: 80 requests per instance
- CPU Boost: Enabled (faster startup)
- Platform: Managed

---

### 9. PORT CONFIGURATIONS

| Component | Port | Environment | Purpose |
|-----------|------|-----------|---------|
| Frontend (Nuxt dev) | 3000 | Development only | Vue.js dev server |
| Backend (Express) | 3002 | Development | API server |
| Backend (Express) | 8080 | Production | API server in Docker |
| Health Check | 8080/health | All | Container health probing |
| API Routes | 8080/api/* | All | REST API endpoints |
| Swagger UI | 8080/docs | All | Interactive API documentation |
| Static Frontend | 8080/* | Production | Serves built Nuxt static files |

**Port Usage Notes:**
- Frontend and backend are **separate services** in development
- Frontend proxies `/api/**` requests to backend (via nuxt.config.ts routeRules)
- In production (Docker), **both frontend and backend run in same container on port 8080**
  - Express serves compiled Nuxt static files from `/dist`
  - API routes at `/api`
  - SPA fallback serves index.html for all non-API routes

---

### 10. HEALTH CHECK ENDPOINTS

**Primary Health Check:**
- **Endpoint:** `GET /health`
- **Port:** 8080
- **Expected Response:** HTTP 200 OK
- **Timeout:** 3 seconds
- **Docker HEALTHCHECK:**
  ```
  --interval=30s  # Check every 30 seconds
  --timeout=5s    # Wait max 5 seconds for response
  --start-period=40s  # Give container 40s to start
  --retries=3     # Fail after 3 consecutive failures
  ```

**API Status/Info Endpoints:**
- `GET /api/version` - Returns version info
  ```json
  {
    "version": "0.9.1",
    "commit": "a34f5234",
    "display": "0.9.1 (a34f5234)"
  }
  ```

**Swagger/API Documentation:**
- `GET /docs` - Swagger UI interface
- `GET /docs/swagger.json` - OpenAPI specification

**Startup Validation:**
- `docker-entrypoint.sh` validates all environment variables before starting
- Logs success/warning messages for each configuration category
- Warns if critical variables missing but continues (services degraded)

---

### 11. KEY FEATURES & CAPABILITIES

**Core Features:**
- AI Agent marketplace and catalog
- Agent management (CRUD operations)
- Live agent demos with streaming video
- Use case request system with workflow
- Solutions catalog with PDF generation
- Success stories/case studies
- Training/courses management
- Admin panel with multi-role access control
- Real-time chat with MCP-enabled agents
- RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation) for document-based AI
- File uploads (images, videos, documents)
- Analytics and usage tracking
- Webhook notifications
- Swagger API documentation

**Authentication & Authorization:**
- Google OAuth 2.0 (internal Konecta domain only)
- Cookie-based JWT session tokens
- Role-based access control (RBAC) with 7 roles
- Email domain restriction (@konecta.com)
- Admin configuration panel for managing allowed domains

**AI Integrations:**
- Google Vertex AI (Gemini LLM inference)
- Model Context Protocol (MCP) for external tools
- Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) with corpus
- Chat history with context management

---

### 12. NOTABLE CONFIGURATION DETAILS

**Frontend Build & Proxying:**
- Nuxt runs in **SPA (Single Page Application) mode** (ssr: false)
- Frontend proxies `/api/**` to backend during dev
- Nuxt generates static files in `.output/public`
- Static files copied to `/dist` in Docker
- Express serves static files + SPA fallback in production

**Database Migrations:**
- Auto-run on backend startup
- 15+ SQL migrations tracked in `/backend/db/migrations/`
- Manual control via npm scripts
- No ORM - all SQL queries are raw/hand-written

**TypeScript Configuration:**
- Strict mode enabled
- Separate configs for app (tsconfig.app.json) and build tools (tsconfig.node.json)
- All backend code executed via `tsx` (TypeScript execution)

**Docker Security:**
- Non-root user (nodejs:1001)
- Read-only file system (chmod 555 on app files)
- No shell/bash in final image (Alpine base)
- Minimal attack surface

**Testing:**
- Playwright for end-to-end testing
- Test files in `/backend/tests/`

**File Uploads:**
- Memory-based storage (via multer memoryStorage)
- Direct upload to Google Cloud Storage
- 100MB default limit
- Signed URLs generated for secure access
- Public buckets for images/videos, signed URLs for RAG documents

---

### Summary

This is a **modern, production-grade full-stack web application** built with:
- **Nuxt 4/Vue 3** frontend for rich UI
- **Express.js** backend API
- **PostgreSQL** for persistent data
- **Google Cloud** infrastructure (Cloud Run, Cloud SQL, Storage, AI services)
- **Advanced AI/ML features** (Vertex AI, RAG, MCP support)
- **Automated CI/CD** via Google Cloud Build
- **Enterprise-grade authentication** (Google OAuth + JWT)
- **Multi-environment support** (dev/staging/prod)
- **Comprehensive API documentation** (Swagger)

The architecture separates concerns cleanly with a stateless API backend and independent frontend, enabling horizontal scaling and independent deployments on Cloud Run.

## COMPREHENSIVE IQ PORTAL SERVICE MIGRATION GUIDE & SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION

I have thoroughly explored the `/tmp/kix-platform-docs` repository and read **ALL** content related to the IQ Portal service migration guide. Below is the complete content organized by topic:

---

## PART 1: IQ PORTAL MIGRATION GUIDE (Main Document)

**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/service-guides/iq-portal.md`

### Overview
- **Service**: kon-iq-portal
- **Code Repo**: `konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal`
- **Workload Repo**: `konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal`
- **Priority**: P0 (Pilot)

### 1. Current State Assessment

**Architecture**: Single-container monolith running Express.js (TypeScript) backend serving both API (`/api/*`) and pre-generated Nuxt 4 SPA (static files from `/dist`). Everything runs on port 8080 in production.

**Current Tech Stack**:
| Component | Detail |
|-----------|--------|
| Runtime | Node.js 22 (Alpine) |
| Frontend | Nuxt 4 (SPA, `ssr: false`, pre-generated static) |
| Backend | Express 5 (TypeScript, via `tsx`) |
| Database | PostgreSQL on Cloud SQL (`kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform`) |
| DB Connection | Unix socket via VPC connector (`DB_HOST=/cloudsql/...`) |
| Migrations | Custom SQL/TS runner (33 migrations, runs on startup) |
| Auth | Google OAuth → JWT in HttpOnly cookies |
| Storage | GCS (3 buckets: images, videos, RAG docs) |
| AI | Vertex AI (Gemini) + RAG corpus |
| Async Jobs | Cloud Tasks → kon-rag-ingestion |
| Email | ChannelHub (external Cognito-auth API) |
| CI/CD | Cloud Build from Bitbucket |

**Current GCP Service Dependencies**:
| Service | Project | Used For |
|---------|---------|----------|
| Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) | `kd-lab-464110` | Primary database |
| Cloud Storage | `kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev` | Image/video/RAG file storage |
| Vertex AI | `kd-lab-464110` | Gemini LLM + RAG retrieval |
| Cloud Tasks | `kd-lab-464110` | Async RAG document ingestion |
| Secret Manager | `kd-lab-464110` | JWT secret, DB password, RAG token |

### 2. Target State Architecture

**Target Deployment**:
- **Cluster**: kix-infrastructure GKE Cluster
- **Namespace**: `dev-iqportal` (then `qa-iqportal`, `prod-iqportal`)
- **Pod**: `kix-apps-konectaiq` with 2 containers:
  - **app** (Express.js :8080) serving API + SPA
  - **cloud-sql-proxy** sidecar (:5432 TCP) for database connectivity
- **Ingress**: Dual path:
  - **Kong**: `/api/iq-portal/*` for API consumers
  - **GKE Gateway API + IAP**: `iq.app.ix.konecta-digital.com` for browser users

**Key Differences from Cloud Run**:

| Aspect | Cloud Run | kix-infrastructure |
|--------|-----------|-------------------|
| DB connection | Unix socket via VPC connector | Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar (TCP `localhost:5432`) |
| Secrets | Secret Manager direct access | External Secrets Operator → K8s Secrets |
| Scaling | 0-10 instances | HPA 1-5 replicas |
| Ingress | Direct HTTPS | Dual: Kong (API) + Gateway API + IAP (frontend) |
| CI/CD | Cloud Build + Bitbucket | GitHub Actions + ArgoCD |
| Health checks | Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK | K8s liveness/readiness probes |

### 3. Pre-requisites

Before starting, ensure:
- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-applications` GitHub org (write) — for code repo
- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-workloads` GitHub org (write) — for workload repo
- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-applications/.github-private` (read) — shared CI workflows
- [ ] `kix-platform-workload-template` available for scaffolding workload repos
- [ ] Shared Kong gateway operational in `shared-kong` namespace
- [ ] GKE Gateway API + IAP configured on frontend LB
- [ ] GCP Workload Identity pool configured for GitHub Actions OIDC
- [ ] Backstage operational in `shared-backstage` namespace
- [ ] Cloud SQL instance accessible from GKE cluster (private IP, same VPC)
- [ ] GCP service account for IQ Portal workload with IAM roles:
  - `roles/cloudsql.client`
  - `roles/storage.objectAdmin`
  - `roles/aiplatform.user`
  - `roles/cloudtasks.enqueuer`
  - `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor`

### STEP-BY-STEP MIGRATION PROCESS

### Step 1: Create the Repositories

**Code Repository**:
```bash
gh repo create konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal \
  --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template \
  --private

git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal.git
cd kix-app-iqportal
```

**Workload Repository**:
```bash
gh repo create konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal \
  --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-workload-template \
  --private
```

**Code Repo Directory Structure**:
```
konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal/
├── backend/              # Express.js API
├── src/                  # Nuxt 4 frontend
├── public/               # Static assets
├── scripts/              # Deployment scripts
├── nuxt.config.ts
├── package.json
├── package-lock.json
├── tsconfig*.json
├── version.txt
├── Dockerfile            # Modified (see Step 3)
├── docker-entrypoint.sh
├── .dockerignore
├── catalog-info.yaml     # Backstage descriptor
├── docs/                 # TechDocs content
│   └── index.md
├── .github/workflows/    # CI pipeline
│   └── ci.yaml
├── CODEOWNERS
└── .env.example
```

**Workload Repo Directory Structure**:
```
konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal/
├── helm/                 # Helm chart
│   ├── Chart.yaml
│   ├── values.yaml
│   ├── values-dev.yaml
│   ├── values-qa.yaml
│   └── values-prod.yaml
├── argocd/
│   └── application.yaml  # ArgoCD Application manifest
├── terraform/            # Service infrastructure
│   ├── main.tf
│   ├── variables.tf
│   └── backend.tf
├── catalog-info.yaml     # Backstage service catalog descriptor
├── .github/workflows/
│   └── deploy.yaml       # Terraform workflow
└── CODEOWNERS
```

**Add to Code Repo**:

1. **CODEOWNERS**:
```
* @konecta-ix-workloads/iq-portal-team
```

2. **.dockerignore**:
```
node_modules
.nuxt
.output
dist
.git
.github
chart
docs
*.md
.env*
!.env.example
```

3. **catalog-info.yaml**:
```yaml
apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1
kind: Component
metadata:
  name: kix-app-iqportal
  description: Konecta IQ Portal — AI-powered knowledge platform
  annotations:
    github.com/project-slug: konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal
    argocd/app-name: kix-app-iqportal
    backstage.io/kubernetes-id: kix-app-iqportal
    backstage.io/kubernetes-namespace: dev-iqportal,qa-iqportal,prod-iqportal
    backstage.io/techdocs-ref: dir:.
    sonarqube.org/project-key: kix-app-iqportal
  tags:
    - nodejs
    - express
    - nuxt
    - typescript
    - cloud-sql
spec:
  type: service
  lifecycle: production
  owner: group:iq-portal-team
  system: kix-platform
  dependsOn:
    - resource:cloud-sql-iqplatform
    - component:kix-services-kong
    - component:kix-mcp-gateway
  links:
    - url: https://github.com/konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal
      title: Workload Repo
      icon: github
```

---

### Step 2: Application Code Changes

**2.1 Database Connection**

No code changes required. The existing `database.ts` already supports both Unix socket (Cloud Run) and TCP (K8s):

```typescript
// backend/db/database.ts — already handles both
const pool = process.env.DATABASE_URL
    ? new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL })
    : new Pool({
        host: process.env.DB_HOST || 'localhost',
        port: parseInt(process.env.DB_PORT || '5432'),
        // ...
    });
```

On kix, environment variables will be:
```
DB_HOST=localhost        # Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar
DB_PORT=5432
```

**2.2 Add Root-Level Health Endpoint**

Add to `backend/routes/index.ts`:

```typescript
// K8S HEALTH PROBES
// Lightweight endpoint for Kubernetes liveness probes
app.get('/health', (req, res) => {
    res.status(200).json({ status: 'ok' });
});
```

Keep `/api/health` for detailed checks (DB + GCP connectivity).

**2.3 Environment Variables**

All changes are via Helm values, no code changes:

| Variable | kix Value |
|----------|-----------|
| `DB_HOST` | `localhost` (sidecar) |
| `DB_PORT` | `5432` |
| `MCP_GATEWAY_URL` | `http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/mcp-gateway` |
| `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | `https://iq-portal.app.ix.konecta-digital.com` |

---

### Step 3: Dockerfile Changes

**3.1 Pin Base Image Digests**

Replace all `FROM node:22-alpine` with pinned digests:

```dockerfile
FROM node:22-alpine@sha256:<current-digest> AS dependencies
```

**3.2 Remove HEALTHCHECK Instruction**

Delete the `HEALTHCHECK` block (lines 142-157) — K8s probes replace it.

---

### Step 4: Helm Chart (Workload Repo)

Configure the Helm chart in `konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal/helm/`.

**4.1 Chart.yaml**:

```yaml
apiVersion: v2
name: kix-workload-iqportal
version: 0.1.0
type: application
```

**4.2 values.yaml**:

```yaml
nameOverride: kix-app-iqportal

image:
  repository: europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-shr-artifacts/approved-images/kix-app-iqportal
  tag: "latest"  # Overridden by CI

containers:
  app:
    port: 8080
    env:
      NODE_ENV: "production"
      PORT: "8080"
      DB_HOST: "localhost"
      DB_PORT: "5432"
      DB_NAME: "iqplatform"
      GCP_PROJECT_ID: "kd-ix-<region>-<env>-gke"
      GCP_LOCATION: "<location>"
      VERTEX_AI_PROJECT_ID: "kd-ix-<region>-<env>-ai"
      VERTEX_AI_LOCATION: "<location>"
      VERTEX_AI_MODEL: "<model>"
      RAG_PROJECT_ID: "<rag project>"
      RAG_LOCATION: "<rag project>"
      RAG_CORPUS_ID: "<rag corpus>"
      MCP_GATEWAY_URL: "http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/mcp-gateway"
      CLOUD_TASKS_PROJECT_ID: "<CLOUD_TASKS project>"
      CLOUD_TASKS_QUEUE: "<CLOUD_TASKS queue>"
      CLOUD_TASKS_LOCATION: "<CLOUD_TASKS location>"
      CHANNELHUB_URL: "https://api.konecta.cloud/one-channel-hub/prd"
      CHANNELHUB_LOGIN_URL: "https://ch-prd.auth.eu-west-1.amazoncognito.com"
      LOG_LEVEL: "info"
    envFrom:
      - secretRef:
          name: kix-app-iqportal-secrets
    resources:
      requests:
        cpu: 250m
        memory: 256Mi
      limits:
        cpu: "1"
        memory: 512Mi

cloudSqlProxy:
  enabled: true
  instanceConnectionName: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform"
  port: 5432

serviceAccount:
  create: true
  annotations:
    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: kix-app-iqportal@kd-ix-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com

probes:
  liveness:
    httpGet:
      path: /health
      port: 8080
    initialDelaySeconds: 30
    periodSeconds: 15
  readiness:
    httpGet:
      path: /api/health
      port: 8080
    initialDelaySeconds: 40
    periodSeconds: 10
  startup:
    httpGet:
      path: /health
      port: 8080
    initialDelaySeconds: 10
    periodSeconds: 5
    failureThreshold: 12

autoscaling:
  enabled: true
  minReplicas: 1
  maxReplicas: 5
  targetCPUUtilization: 70

services:
  api:
    port: 8080
    ingress:
      class: kong
      path: /api/iq-portal
      stripPath: true
  frontend:
    port: 8080
    ingress:
      class: gateway-api
      host: iq.<env>.ix.konecta-digital.com
```

---

### Step 5: GitHub Actions CI Workflow (Code Repo)

Create `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` in the **code repo**:

```yaml
name: CI Pipeline

on:
  push:
    branches: [develop, main]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]

permissions:
  contents: read
  id-token: write
  security-events: write

jobs:
  pipeline:
    uses: konecta-ix-applications/.github-private/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@main
    with:
      service-name: kix-app-iqportal
      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile
      node-version: "22"
      build-args: |
        VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=${{ vars.VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
        NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=${{ vars.ENVIRONMENT }}
        COMMIT_SHA=${{ github.sha }}
      skip-tests: true
    secrets: inherit
```

**Note**: No `chart-path` — Helm charts live in the workload repo, not the code repo.

---

### Step 6: Secrets Migration (Workload Repo)

Secrets are managed via **Terraform** in the workload repo (`kix-workload-iqportal/terraform/`).

**6.1 Auto-Generated Secrets (Terraform)**

Add to `terraform/main.tf`:

```hcl
# Auto-generated secrets
resource "random_password" "db" {
  length  = 32
  special = true
}

resource "random_password" "jwt" {
  length  = 64
  special = true
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "db_password" {
  secret_id = "kix-iq-portal-db-password-${var.environment}"
  replication { auto {} }
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "db_password" {
  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id
  secret_data = random_password.db.result
}
```

**6.2 Static Secrets (GitHub Actions → Terraform)**

Add per-environment secrets to the **workload repo** GitHub settings:

| GitHub Secret (per env) | Terraform Variable | Source |
|-------------------------|-------------------|--------|
| `TF_VARS_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` | `var.google_client_id` | Google OAuth |
| `TF_VARS_RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN` | `var.rag_pipeline_token` | RAG service |
| `TF_VARS_CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID` | `var.channelhub_client_id` | ChannelHub |
| `TF_VARS_CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` | `var.channelhub_client_secret` | ChannelHub |
| `TF_VARS_CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID` | `var.channelhub_connection_id` | ChannelHub |

**6.3 Create Kubernetes Secret (Terraform)**

```hcl
resource "kubernetes_secret" "app_secrets" {
  metadata {
    name      = "kix-app-iqportal-secrets"
    namespace = var.namespace
  }
  data = {
    DB_PASSWORD               = random_password.db.result
    DB_USER                   = var.db_user
    JWT_SECRET                = random_password.jwt.result
    GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID          = var.google_client_id
    RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN        = var.rag_pipeline_token
    CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID      = var.channelhub_client_id
    CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET  = var.channelhub_client_secret
    CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID  = var.channelhub_connection_id
    GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET          = var.gcs_image_bucket
    GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET          = var.gcs_video_bucket
    GCS_RAG_BUCKET            = var.gcs_rag_bucket
  }
}
```

**6.4 Apply Terraform**

```bash
cd kix-workload-iqportal/terraform
terraform init
terraform plan \
  -var="environment=dev" \
  -var="google_client_id=$TF_VARS_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID" \
  -var="rag_pipeline_token=$TF_VARS_RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN" \
  # ... other TF_VARS_
terraform apply
```

---

### Step 7: GitOps Configuration (Workload Repo)

**7.1 ArgoCD Application**

Create `argocd/application.yaml` in the workload repo:

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: kix-app-iqportal-dev
  namespace: shared-argocd
spec:
  project: dev-project
  source:
    repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal
    path: helm
    targetRevision: main
    helm:
      valueFiles:
        - values.yaml
        - values-dev.yaml
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: dev-iqportal
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
```

**7.2 Dev Environment Values**

Create `helm/values-dev.yaml` in the workload repo:

```yaml
image:
  tag: "sha-<initial-commit>"

containers:
  app:
    env:
      NODE_ENV: "development"
      NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT: "development"
      ALLOWED_ORIGINS: "https://iq-portal.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
      LOG_LEVEL: "debug"

autoscaling:
  minReplicas: 1
  maxReplicas: 2

services:
  frontend:
    ingress:
      host: iq-portal.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com
```

---

### Step 8: Database Migration

**Strategy**: The app runs migrations automatically on startup. This is safe because:
- The Cloud SQL instance already exists with the schema
- Migrations are idempotent (tracked in `schema_migrations` table)
- No schema changes are part of this migration

**Connectivity Test**:

```bash
kubectl run sql-test --rm -it \
  --image=gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.14.3 \
  --namespace=dev-iqportal \
  --serviceaccount=kix-apps-konectaiq \
  -- --private-ip kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform
```

---

### Step 9: Verification & Smoke Tests

**9.1 Deployment Checklist**:

```bash
NAMESPACE=dev-iqportal

# 1. Pod running (2/2 = app + cloud-sql-proxy)
kubectl get pods -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-app-iqportal

# 2. Both containers healthy
kubectl describe pod -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-app-iqportal | grep -A5 "Conditions"

# 3. Logs check
kubectl logs -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-app-iqportal -c app | grep "running on"

# 4. Cloud SQL proxy connected
kubectl logs -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-app-iqportal -c cloud-sql-proxy | grep "ready"
```

**9.2 Functional Smoke Tests**:

```bash
BASE_URL="https://iq-portal.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"

# Health check
curl -s $BASE_URL/health | jq .
# Expected: {"status":"ok"}

# Full health check
curl -s $BASE_URL/api/health | jq .
# Expected: {"status":"ok","checks":{"database":"ok","gcp":"ok"}}

# Version
curl -s $BASE_URL/api/version | jq .

# Frontend loads
curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" $BASE_URL/
# Expected: 200

# API auth check
curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" $BASE_URL/api/agents
# Expected: 401
```

---

### Step 10: DNS & Domain Cutover

**New Domains**:

| Path | Domain |
|------|--------|
| Frontend (browser) | `iq-portal.app.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| Frontend (dev) | `iq-portal.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| API (machine) | `api.ix.konecta-digital.com/api/iq-portal` |

**Decommission Cloud Run**:

Once verified:

```bash
# Stop Cloud Run
gcloud run services update kon-iq-portal \
  --region=europe-west1 \
  --max-instances=0

# After confidence period (1-2 weeks), delete
gcloud run services delete kon-iq-portal \
  --region=europe-west1
```

| `DB_PORT` | Default (5432) | Helm `env` |
| `DB_NAME` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `DB_USER` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `DB_PASSWORD` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `JWT_SECRET` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `GCP_PROJECT_ID` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `GCP_LOCATION` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `VERTEX_AI_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `RAG_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `GCS_*_BUCKET` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |
| `CLOUD_TASKS_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |
| `MCP_GATEWAY_URL` | Default | Helm `env` |

### APPENDIX B: Rollback Procedure

**Via Git**:

```bash
cd kix-workload-iqportal
git revert HEAD
git push
# ArgoCD syncs automatically
```

**Via kubectl**:

```bash
kubectl rollout undo deployment/kix-app-iqportal -n dev-iqportal
```

**Fall back to Cloud Run**:

```bash
gcloud run services update kon-iq-portal \
  --region=europe-west1 \
  --max-instances=10
```

## PART 2: SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION

### A. Application Migration Index Overview
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/index.md`

The platform is migrating approximately **24 microservices** from Cloud Run to kix-infrastructure Kubernetes platform. This delivers:
- Standardised packaging with Helm charts
- Secure container builds with multi-stage Docker, vulnerability scanning, and image signing
- Unified CI/CD via GitHub Actions for build/scan/push and ArgoCD for GitOps
- Developer portal via Backstage for service catalog, scaffolding, and documentation
- Dual ingress architecture with Kong for APIs and GKE Gateway API + IAP for frontends

**Key principles**: Simple, secure, auditable, repeatable.

---

### B. Target Architecture
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/target-architecture.md`

**High-Level Architecture**:
```
Internet ──────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────
         (HTTPS API)                           │ (HTTPS Frontend)
              │                                 │
              ▼                                 ▼
        GCP LB + Cloud Armor           GCP LB + Gateway API + IAP
        (API ingress via Kong)         (Frontend ingress)
              │                                 │
              └──────────┬──────────────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
        KIX-INFRASTRUCTURE GKE CLUSTER
        ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ shared-kong (API Gateway)          │
        │ shared-litellm (LLM Proxy)         │
        │ shared-backstage (Dev Portal)      │
        │ shared-monitoring (Observability)  │
        │                                    │
        │ Application Namespaces:            │
        │ {env}-{workload}                   │
        │ (e.g. dev-iqportal)                │
        └────────────────────────────────────┘
              │
              ▼ (via Workload Identity)
        ┌─────────────────────┐
        │ Cloud SQL           │
        │ Vertex AI / GCS     │
        │ Secret Manager      │
        └─────────────────────┘
```

**Repository Model** (Two-Repo Pattern):

1. **Code Repositories** (konecta-ix-applications, konecta-ix-mcp-servers):
   - Only application source code
   - Dockerfile
   - CI pipeline (.github/workflows/ci.yaml)
   - Backstage catalog descriptor

2. **Workload Repositories** (konecta-ix-workloads):
   - Helm charts
   - ArgoCD Application manifests
   - Terraform infrastructure
   - No application source code

**Secrets Management** (Terraform-First Approach):

1. **Auto-Generated Secrets**: Terraform creates (database passwords, API keys)
2. **Static Secrets**: GitHub Actions secrets prefixed with `TF_VARS_`, passed to Terraform

---

### C. Kubernetes & Helm
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/kubernetes-helm.md`

**Namespace Strategy**: 
- Each workload gets its own namespace per environment: `{env}-{workload}`
- Example: `dev-iqportal`, `qa-iqportal`, `prod-iqportal`

**Resource Profiles**:

| Profile | CPU Request | CPU Limit | Mem Request | Mem Limit | Use Case |
|---------|-------------|-----------|-------------|-----------|----------|
| **API Service** | 100m | 500m | 256Mi | 512Mi | FastAPI, MCP servers, bots, pipelines |
| **API Heavy** | 250m | 1000m | 512Mi | 1Gi | RAG, document processing, ML |
| **Frontend** | 50m | 200m | 128Mi | 256Mi | Nuxt 3 SSR |

**Health Probes**:

| Service Type | Liveness | Readiness |
|--------------|----------|-----------|
| **FastAPI** | `GET /healthz` :8000 | `GET /readyz` :8000 (checks DB) |
| **Express.js** | `GET /health` :8080 | `GET /api/health` :8080 |
| **Nuxt 3** | `GET /` :3000 | `GET /_health` :3000 |

**Autoscaling (HPA)**:

| Tier | Min | Max | Target CPU |
|------|-----|-----|------------|
| **User-facing** | 2 | 20 | 60% |
| **Internal APIs, MCP servers** | 2 | 10 | 75% |
| **Data pipelines, bots** | 1 | 5 | 80% |

**Cloud SQL Auth Proxy Sidecar**:
- Per-pod Workload Identity
- Least-privilege IAM per service
- Scales with application pods
- Connection at `localhost:5432` (TCP)

**Multi-Tier App Pattern** (Frontend + Backend):
- Nuxt frontend (port 3000) + FastAPI backend (port 8000) + cloud-sql-proxy (5432)
- Deploy as single pod with multiple containers
- Frontend calls backend via `localhost:8000`
- Both containers deploy atomically

---

### D. Ingress & Networking
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/ingress-networking.md`

**Dual Load Balancer Architecture**:

1. **API Path** (Kong Ingress):
   - Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor → Kong → {env}-{workload} namespaces
   - Machine-to-machine, API consumers
   - Kong handles rate limiting, auth, CORS

2. **Frontend Path** (GKE Gateway API + IAP):
   - Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor + Gateway API + IAP → {env}-{workload} namespaces
   - Browser users, human authentication via Google/OIDC

**Network Policies**:

1. **Default Deny Ingress**: All namespaces have default deny
2. **GCP Health Check IPs**: MANDATORY for load balancer services
   - 35.191.0.0/16
   - 130.211.0.0/22
3. **Allow from Kong**: For API services
4. **Allow from Gateway**: For frontend services
5. **LiteLLM Isolation**: Kong-only access

**DNS by Environment**:

| Environment | API Domain | Frontend Domain |
|-------------|------------|-----------------|
| Dev | `dev.api.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `dev.app.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| QA | `qa.api.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `qa.app.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| Prod | `api.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `app.ix.konecta-digital.com` |

**TLS Management**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt in `shared-cert-manager` namespace

---

### E. Docker Security
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/docker-security.md`

**Base Image Policy**:

| Stack | Builder Stage | Runtime Stage |
|-------|---------------|---------------|
| **Python** | `python:3.x-slim` | `python:3.x-slim` |
| **Node.js** | `node:20-alpine` | `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` |

**Multi-Stage Build Pattern**:

1. Builder stage: Install dependencies, compile, cache build artifacts
2. Runtime stage: Copy only production artifacts, non-root user

**Security Hardening**:
- Non-root user (UID 65532)
- Read-only root filesystem with explicit writable paths (`/tmp`, `/app/.cache`)
- Drop all capabilities
- No shell in production (distroless images)
- No secrets in images (via K8s ConfigMaps/Secrets)

**Dependency Management**:
- Python: Pin with hashes via `pip-compile` or `uv lock`
- Node.js: Always use `npm ci` (never `npm install` in CI)
- Automated updates via Renovate

**OCI Labels** (Build Metadata):
```dockerfile
LABEL org.opencontainers.image.created="${BUILD_DATE}"
LABEL org.opencontainers.image.revision="${GIT_SHA}"
LABEL org.opencontainers.image.version="${VERSION}"
```

---

### F. CI/CD Pipeline
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/cicd-pipeline.md`

**Two-Repo CI/CD Model**:

1. **Code Repos**: Build pipeline (lint, test, build, scan, sign, push Docker images)
2. **Workload Repos**: Deploy pipeline (Terraform apply, ArgoCD sync)

**Shared Workflows** (`.github-private` per org):
```
standard-pipeline.yaml     # Main orchestrator
├── _lint-and-test.yaml    # Language-aware
├── _secret-scan.yaml      # Trivy filesystem
├── _sca.yaml              # Dependency-Track
├── _sast.yaml             # SonarQube
├── _build-image.yaml      # Docker build
├── _container-scan-sign.yaml  # Trivy + Cosign
├── _push-registry.yaml    # Push to AR
└── _promote.yaml          # Between environments
```

**Code Repo CI Workflow** (`.github/workflows/ci.yaml`):

```yaml
name: CI
on:
  push:
    branches: [develop, main]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]

permissions:
  contents: read
  id-token: write
  security-events: write

jobs:
  pipeline:
    uses: konecta-ix-applications/.github-private/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@main
    with:
      service-name: kix-app-iqportal
      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile
      node-version: "22"
    secrets: inherit
```

**Pipeline Stages**:

| Stage | Trigger | Tool | Purpose |
|-------|---------|------|---------|
| **1. Lint & Test** | PR + merge | ruff/eslint, type checks | Fast feedback |
| **2. Secret Scan** | PR + merge | Trivy filesystem | Block secrets before commit |
| **3. SCA** | PR + merge | Dependency-Track | Vulnerability check |
| **4. SAST** | PR + merge | SonarQube | Code analysis |
| **5. Build & Test** | Merge only | Docker BuildKit | Docker build |
| **6. Container Scan** | Merge only | Trivy + Syft + Cosign | Scan + sign |
| **7. Push** | Merge only | Artifact Registry | Push to registry |

**Security Gates** (Fail-Stop):
- **Secret Detection**: Zero tolerance
- **Dependency Vulnerabilities**: Stop on critical/high
- **SAST Quality Gate**: Stop on critical/high
- **Container Vulnerabilities**: Stop on critical/high
- **Image Signature**: Cosign (OIDC keyless)
- **DAST**: OWASP ZAP before prod promotion
- **Admission Control**: Kyverno (rejects unsigned images in prod)

**Image Tagging Strategy**:

| Tag | When | Purpose |
|-----|------|---------|
| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable identifier |
| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | Git tag | Release version |
| `{env}-latest` | On promotion | Environment pointer (mutable) |

**Secrets Management (CI)**:
- **GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity**: No JSON keys
- **Build Secrets**: Via `docker build --secret` (never in layers)
- **Workload Repo Secrets**: `TF_VARS_*` pattern for Terraform

---

### G. GitOps & Promotion
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/gitops-promotion.md`

**GitOps Flow** (Per-Workload Repository Model):

```
CODE REPO                              WORKLOAD REPO
Developer Push → CI builds             ArgoCD watches
  (code-org/kix-app-iqportal)          (kix-workload-iqportal)
       │
       ▼ Pipeline runs
       Image sha-abc1234 pushed
       │
       ▼ Developer/automation updates
       values-{env}.yaml with sha-abc1234
       │
       ▼ Git push
       ArgoCD detects change
       │
       ▼
       Helm template + apply to {env}-iqportal
```

**ArgoCD Configuration**:

Each workload repo contains its own ArgoCD Application manifest:

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: kix-app-iqportal-dev
  namespace: shared-argocd
spec:
  project: dev-project
  source:
    repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-iqportal
    path: helm
    targetRevision: main
    helm:
      valueFiles:
        - values.yaml
        - values-dev.yaml
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: dev-iqportal
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
```

**Sync Policies by Environment**:

| Environment | Sync Mode | When |
|-------------|-----------|------|
| **Dev** | `automated` (auto-sync) | 24/7 |
| **QA** | `automated: false` (manual) | 08:00-18:00 UTC |
| **Prod** | `automated: false` (manual) | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |

**Environment Promotion**:

```
Dev (values-dev.yaml)  →  QA (values-qa.yaml)  →  Prod (values-prod.yaml)
  sha-abc1234                sha-abc1234               sha-abc1234
```

1. **Dev → QA**: Update image tag in values-qa.yaml, create PR, review, merge
2. **QA → Prod**: Run OWASP ZAP baseline scan, if pass, update values-prod.yaml, merge

**Rollback Procedures**:

1. **Via Git** (Recommended): `git revert HEAD && git push`
2. **Via kubectl**: `kubectl rollout undo deployment/kix-app-iqportal -n dev-iqportal`
3. **Via ArgoCD**: `argocd app rollback kix-app-iqportal-dev <revision>`

**Sync Waves**:

| Wave | Resources |
|------|-----------|
| 0 | Namespaces, RBAC, SecretStores |
| 5 | ConfigMaps, Secrets |
| 10 | Deployments, Services, HPA |
| 15 | Ingress, NetworkPolicies |

---

### H. Security Architecture
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/security.md`

**Pod Security Standards** (All pods: `restricted` profile):

```yaml
podSecurityContext:
  runAsNonRoot: true
  runAsUser: 65532
  fsGroup: 65532

containerSecurityContext:
  allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
  readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
  capabilities:
    drop: [ALL]
```

**Secrets Management**:
- **Auto-Generated**: Terraform creates passwords, keys
- **Static**: GitHub `TF_VARS_*` secrets → Terraform → K8s Secrets
- **Rotation**: Re-run Terraform, rolling pod restart

**Workload Identity** (No Service Account Keys):

```yaml
serviceAccount:
  annotations:
    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: kix-mcp-gateway@kd-ix-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com
```

**Least Privilege IAM Roles**:

| Service Type | Required Roles |
|--------------|----------------|
| **API with Cloud SQL** | `roles/cloudsql.client` |
| **With GCS access** | `roles/storage.objectViewer` or `objectAdmin` |
| **With Vertex AI** | `roles/aiplatform.user` |
| **With Pub/Sub** | `roles/pubsub.publisher` or `subscriber` |
| **With Secret Manager** | `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor` |

**Network Security**:
- Default deny ingress on all namespaces
- Explicit allow rules for GCP health checks, Kong, Gateway API
- LiteLLM Kong-only access

**Admission Control** (Kyverno):

| Policy | Effect |
|--------|--------|
| `require-image-signature` | Reject unsigned images (prod) |
| `disallow-latest-tag` | Block `:latest` tag |
| `require-resource-limits` | Enforce CPU/memory limits |
| `require-konecta-registry` | Only Artifact Registry images |
| `restrict-capabilities` | Enforce capability drop |
| `require-labels` | Enforce standard labels |

**Audit Logging**:
- GCP Admin Activity (always on)
- GCP Data Access (enabled for sensitive services)
- K8s audit logs to Cloud Logging
- Retention: 30 days (hot), 7 years (archive)

**Compliance**:
- EU Data Residency (europe-west1, europe-west4 only)
- Domain-restricted IAM (`konecta-group.com` only)
- No service account key downloads (enforced)
- Audit logging mandatory (enforced)

---

### I. Observability
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/observability.md`

**Observability Stack**:

| Component | Tool | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| **Metrics** | Prometheus | Scrape K8s + application metrics |
| **Dashboards** | Grafana | Visualisation, alerting |
| **Tracing** | Jaeger | Distributed traces (OpenTelemetry) |
| **Logging** | GCP Cloud Logging | Centralised aggregation |
| **Instrumentation** | OpenTelemetry SDK | App-level traces, metrics, logs |

**Metrics** (FastAPI Example):

```python
from prometheus_client import Counter, Histogram

REQUEST_COUNT = Counter('http_requests_total', 'Total HTTP requests', 
                        ['method', 'endpoint', 'status'])
REQUEST_LATENCY = Histogram('http_request_duration_seconds', 'HTTP request latency')

@app.get("/metrics")
async def metrics():
    return Response(generate_latest(), media_type="text/plain")
```

**Distributed Tracing** (OpenTelemetry):

```python
from opentelemetry import trace
from opentelemetry.instrumentation.fastapi import FastAPIInstrumentor
from opentelemetry.exporter.otlp.proto.grpc.trace_exporter import OTLPSpanExporter

trace.set_tracer_provider(TracerProvider())
otlp_exporter = OTLPSpanExporter(endpoint="otel-collector.shared-monitoring:4317")
trace.get_tracer_provider().add_span_processor(BatchSpanProcessor(otlp_exporter))
FastAPIInstrumentor.instrument_app(app)
```

**Structured Logging** (JSON):

```json
{
  "timestamp": "2026-02-10T12:00:00Z",
  "level": "info",
  "event": "request_processed",
  "request_id": "abc-123",
  "duration_ms": 45,
  "status_code": 200,
  "user_id": "user-456"
}
```

**Standard Dashboards**:
- Platform Overview (cluster health, resources)
- Service Health (per-service request rate, latency, errors)
- Kong Gateway (API traffic, rate limiting)
- LiteLLM Proxy (LLM request rate, token usage)
- Database Connections (Cloud SQL pool, query latency)

**Alerting** (PrometheusRule):

| Alert | Condition | Severity |
|-------|-----------|----------|
| **Pod CrashLoopBackOff** | > 2 restarts in 5m | Critical |
| **High Error Rate** | > 5% for 3m | Critical |
| **High Latency** | P95 > 2x baseline for 5m | Warning |
| **HPA at Max** | Max replicas for > 10m | Warning |
| **Certificate Expiry** | < 14 days | Warning |

**SLOs**:

| SLI | Target | Measurement |
|-----|--------|-------------|
| **Availability** | 99.9% | Successful / total requests |
| **Latency** | P95 < 500ms | Request duration histogram |
| **Error Rate** | < 0.1% | 5xx / total responses |
| **Saturation** | CPU < 80% | Container CPU utilisation |

---

### J. Migration Phases
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/migration-phases.md`

**Migration Priority**:

| Priority | Services | Rationale |
|----------|----------|-----------|
| **P0 — Pilot** | kix-app-iqportal | Validates entire platform: both ingress paths, Cloud SQL sidecar, standard pipeline |
| **P1 — Platform Core** | Kong, LiteLLM, MCP Gateway | Must be running before other services |
| **P2 — MCP Servers** | Remaining 13 MCP servers | Foundation for AI workflows |
| **P3 — Business Apps** | kix-app-hrfraud, kix-app-hrportal | Remaining user-facing applications |
| **P4 — Data & Demos** | RAG pipelines, bots, prototypes | Lower priority |

**Phase 1: Platform Foundation + Pilot**

Platform tasks:
- Create shared CI workflows per code org
- Create workload template
- Configure ArgoCD
- Install GitHub App for cross-org dispatch
- Configure GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity
- Deploy Kong + LiteLLM
- Deploy Backstage

Pilot (IQ Portal):
- Backend deployment (Kong API, Cloud SQL sidecar, External Secrets)
- Frontend deployment (GKE Gateway API + IAP)
- Helm chart profiles (API Service + Frontend)
- Cross-org dispatch validation
- Full CI pipeline validation

---

### K. Backstage & Onboarding
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/backstage-onboarding.md`

**Backstage Capabilities**:
- Service catalog (auto-discovers `catalog-info.yaml`)
- New service creation via Software Templates
- Documentation rendering (TechDocs)
- CI/CD visibility (GitHub Actions plugin)
- Deployment status (ArgoCD plugin)
- Live K8s status (Kubernetes plugin)
- Security posture (SonarQube + Dependency-Track)
- API documentation (OpenAPI/Swagger)

**Service Onboarding Options**:

1. **Via Backstage Software Template** (Recommended):
   - Open Backstage portal
   - Create Component → Select template
   - Fill in form (name, team, GitHub org, Cloud SQL, ingress type)
   - Backstage scaffolds both code and workload repos
   - `catalog-info.yaml` auto-populated
   - ArgoCD watches workload repo
   - Service appears in Backstage within 30 minutes

2. **Manual Onboarding**:
   - Create code repo from template
   - Create workload repo from template
   - Configure both repos manually
   - Add to Backstage via catalog import

**catalog-info.yaml** (Every Service):

```yaml
apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1
kind: Component
metadata:
  name: kix-app-iqportal
  description: Konecta IQ Portal — AI-powered knowledge platform
  annotations:
    github.com/project-slug: konecta-ix-applications/kix-app-iqportal
    argocd/app-name: kix-app-iqportal
    backstage.io/kubernetes-id: kix-app-iqportal
    backstage.io/kubernetes-namespace: dev-iqportal,qa-iqportal,prod-iqportal
    backstage.io/techdocs-ref: dir:.
    sonarqube.org/project-key: kix-app-iqportal
  tags: [nodejs, express, nuxt, typescript, cloud-sql]
spec:
  type: service
  lifecycle: production
  owner: group:iq-portal-team
  system: kix-platform
  dependsOn:
    - resource:cloud-sql-iqplatform
    - component:kix-services-kong
    - component:kix-mcp-gateway
```

**TechDocs Setup**:

```
service-repo/
├── docs/
│   ├── index.md
│   ├── api.md
│   └── runbook.md
└── mkdocs.yml
```

### N. Operations & Risks
**File**: `/tmp/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/operations-risks.md`

**Cost Considerations**:

| Factor | Cloud Run | GKE (Target) |
|--------|-----------|--------------|
| **Billing** | Per-request (scale to zero) | Reserved capacity (always-on) |
| **Idle cost** | $0 | Node pool minimum |
| **Burst cost** | High (cold starts) | Lower (pre-provisioned) |
| **Predictability** | Variable | Predictable |

**Disaster Recovery**:

| Component | Primary | Secondary |
|-----------|---------|-----------|
| **GKE Cluster** | eu-west1 | eu-west4 (pre-provisioned) |
| **Cloud SQL** | eu-west1 (HA) | Cross-region replica in eu-west4 |
| **GCS Buckets** | Multi-regional (EU) | Inherently replicated |

**Failover Strategy**:
1. Primary health check fails
2. Cloud DNS updates to secondary cluster LB
3. ArgoCD in secondary syncs from same GitOps repo
4. Services running on secondary within 15 minutes
5. **RPO**: Near-zero, **RTO**: < 15 minutes

**Risk Register**:

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|----------|--------|-----------|
| **Resource sizing incorrect** | Medium | Medium | Start generous, tune based on metrics |
| **Secret exposure** | Low | Critical | ESO, no secrets in Git, Workload Identity |
| **ArgoCD sync storms** | Low | Medium | Sync waves, rate limiting, progressive rollout |
| **Team unfamiliarity** | Medium | Medium | Pilot training, runbook documentation |
| **Kong misconfiguration** | Low | High | Test routes before Cloud Run decommission |
| **Cloud SQL connection exhaustion** | Low | High | Connection pooling, per-pod sidecar |
| **Unsigned image deployed** | Low | High | Kyverno admission control |
| **Compromised CI pipeline** | Low | Critical | OIDC auth, minimal permissions |

**Emergency Procedures**:
- Scale service to zero
- Delete ArgoCD app
- Emergency secret rotation
- Database failover
- Full rollback via Git revert or kubectl rollback

## Context Summary

**The Application** (`kix-apps-konectaiq`): A full-stack Node.js 22 / Nuxt 4 + Express 5 monolith serving an AI Agent Marketplace. Currently deployed on Cloud Run with Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL), Vertex AI, GCS, Cloud Tasks, and ChannelHub integrations.

**The Target** (`kix-workload-konectaiq`): An empty workload repo that needs to contain all infrastructure-as-code to deploy this application onto the kix-infrastructure GKE cluster.

## COMPREHENSIVE REPOSITORY EXPLORATION REPORT

I've thoroughly explored the KIX Platform repositories and mapped out the complete workload template structure and available Terraform modules. Here's my complete findings:

---

## PART 1: WORKLOAD TEMPLATE STRUCTURE

### Template Repository Status
The `kix-platform-workload-template` repository **does not exist as a cloneable repo** (returns "Could not resolve to a Repository"), but its **expected structure is well-documented** in the platform docs. Based on the Backstage onboarding documentation and module patterns, here's what a workload repo should contain:

### Expected Workload Repo Directory Structure

```
kix-workload-{service-name}/
├── helm/                          # Helm charts for Kubernetes deployment
│   ├── Chart.yaml                 # Helm chart metadata
│   ├── values.yaml                # Default values
│   ├── values-dev.yaml            # Dev environment overrides
│   ├── values-qa.yaml             # QA environment overrides
│   ├── values-prod.yaml           # Prod environment overrides
│   └── templates/                 # Kubernetes resource templates
│       ├── deployment.yaml
│       ├── service.yaml
│       ├── configmap.yaml
│       └── ...
│
├── argocd/                        # GitOps configuration
│   └── application.yaml           # ArgoCD Application manifest
│
├── terraform/                     # Infrastructure as Code
│   ├── main.tf                    # Resource definitions
│   ├── variables.tf               # Input variables
│   ├── outputs.tf                 # Output values
│   ├── versions.tf                # Provider/TF version constraints
│   └── [service-specific configs] # Service accounts, IAM, secrets
│
├── .github/workflows/             # Deployment automation
│   └── deploy.yaml                # Terraform apply + Helm deployment workflow
│
├── catalog-info.yaml              # Backstage service catalog metadata
├── README.md                       # Documentation
└── .gitignore
```

### Key Workload Repo Components

**1. Helm Configuration (`helm/`)**
- **Chart.yaml**: Metadata (name, version, appVersion, description)
- **values.yaml**: Default configuration (replicas, image, ports, resources)
- **values-{env}.yaml**: Environment-specific overrides (namespace, domains, secrets)
- **templates/**: Kubernetes manifests (Deployment, Service, ConfigMap, Ingress, NetworkPolicy, etc.)

**2. ArgoCD Configuration (`argocd/`)**
- **application.yaml**: ArgoCD Application spec that:
  - Points to Git repo containing Helm charts
  - Specifies target cluster and namespace
  - Defines sync policy (auto-sync enabled)
  - Maps environment branches to namespaces: `develop` → `dev-{service}`, `main` → `qa-{service}`, `v*` tags → `prod-{service}`

**3. Terraform Configuration (`terraform/`)**
- Service account creation for Workload Identity
- IAM bindings (Cloud SQL, Secret Manager, GCS, monitoring)
- Secrets configuration (TF_VARS_* pattern)
- Cross-project access setup

**4. CI/CD Workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yaml`)**
- Triggered on: PR to main, push to main, manual trigger, tags
- Steps:
  1. Checkout code
  2. Setup Terraform
  3. Run `terraform init`
  4. Run `terraform plan` (show changes)
  5. Run `terraform apply` (requires approval in prod)
  6. Run Helm commands (template, validate, deploy)
  7. Sync ArgoCD Application

**5. Backstage Metadata (`catalog-info.yaml`)**
```yaml
apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1
kind: Component
metadata:
  name: {service-name}
  description: {service description}
  annotations:
    github.com/project-slug: {org}/{code-repo}
    argocd/app-name: {service-name}
    backstage.io/kubernetes-id: {service-name}
    backstage.io/kubernetes-namespace: dev-{service},qa-{service},prod-{service}
spec:
  type: service
  lifecycle: production
  owner: group:{team-name}
  system: kix-platform
```

---

## PART 2: AVAILABLE TERRAFORM MODULES

### Complete Module Catalog

**Location:** `/tmp/kix-platform-modules/terraform/`

#### GCP Infrastructure Modules (`terraform/gcp/`)

| Module | Purpose | Key Features |
|--------|---------|--------------|
| **base-environment** | GCP folder hierarchy, project creation | Folder org, project setup, billing |
| **cloud-armor** | WAF security policy | OWASP rules, DDoS protection, Cloud CDN |
| **github-actions-oidc** | GitHub Actions federation | Workload Identity for CI/CD |
| **gke-autopilot** | GKE Autopilot cluster | Auto-managed control plane, autoscaling |
| **gke-cluster** | GKE Standard cluster | Full control, custom node pools |
| **landing-zone** | VPC, NAT, DNS, firewall | Network foundation, Private Google Access |
| **public-dns** | Cloud DNS zones | Domain management |
| **secret-manager** | Secret Manager with rotation | 90-day rotation, audit logging |
| **service-accounts** | Service accounts + Workload Identity | IAM bindings, least privilege |
| **ssl-certificates** | Google-managed SSL certs | DNS validation, auto-renewal |
| **vertex-ai** | Vertex AI config | Workbenches, pipelines |
| **workload-identity** | Workload Identity binding | K8s-to-GCP federation |

#### GCP Data Services Modules (`terraform/gcp/data-services/`)

| Module | Purpose | Key Features |
|--------|---------|--------------|
| **cloud-sql** | PostgreSQL instance | HA with failover, private IP only |
| **cloud-sql-db** | Databases + users | Per-database configuration |
| **gcs** | Google Cloud Storage buckets | Lifecycle policies, regional |
| **pubsub** | Pub/Sub topics/subscriptions | Message queues, DLQ support |
| **redis** | Memorystore Redis | Memory cache, private network |

#### Kubernetes Modules (`terraform/kubernetes/`)

| Module | Purpose | Key Features |
|--------|---------|--------------|
| **argocd** | ArgoCD deployment | GitOps, multi-source apps |
| **gke-config** | Namespaces, RBAC, network policies | **Most Important for workloads** |
| **kong-gateway** | Kong API Gateway | Helm-based, routing rules |
| **litellm** | LiteLLM proxy | LLM request router, caching |
| **litellm-config** | LiteLLM configuration | Model providers, rate limiting |

---

## PART 3: CRITICAL MODULE FOR WORKLOADS - GKE Config

The **`terraform/kubernetes/gke-config`** module is the most important for workload setup. It creates:

### What gke-config Creates

**1. Namespaces**
```
dev-{service}      # Development namespace
qa-{service}       # QA namespace  
prod-{service}     # Production namespace
shared-kong        # Shared Kong gateway
shared-monitoring  # Shared monitoring (Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger)
shared-cert-manager # TLS certificate management
```

**2. Per-Namespace Resources**
- **Resource Quotas**: CPU/memory limits, pod count limits
- **Limit Ranges**: Default CPU/memory per container
- **Network Policies**: Default-deny-ingress, allow Kong access
- **RBAC**: ClusterRoleBindings for admins/developers/viewers
- **Service Accounts**: K8s SA with Workload Identity annotation
- **Pod Security Standards**: baseline/restricted enforcement

**3. Network Isolation Pattern**
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           GKE Cluster                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Kong (gateway) → kube-dns → Google APIs │
│    ↓                                      │
│ [Default-deny network policies]          │
│    ↓                                      │
│ dev-{service} | qa-{service} | prod-{}  │
│ (isolated)    | (isolated)    | (isolated)
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**4. Service Account Configuration**
```hcl
service_account = {
  create              = true
  name                = "myapp-sa"
  gcp_service_account = "myapp@gke-project.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
}
```

---

## PART 4: MODULE DEVELOPMENT PATTERNS

### Standard Module Structure

Every module follows this structure:

```
terraform/gcp/{module-name}/
├── main.tf                          # Resource definitions
├── variables.tf                     # Input variables with validation
├── outputs.tf                       # Output values
├── versions.tf                      # TF & provider constraints (>= 1.5.0)
├── locals.tf                        # Local values (optional)
├── README.md                        # Complete documentation
└── examples/
    ├── basic/                       # Minimal config
    │   ├── main.tf
    │   ├── variables.tf
    │   └── terraform.tfvars.example
    └── production/                  # Full-featured config
        ├── main.tf
        ├── variables.tf
        └── terraform.tfvars.example
```

### Required Module Files

**1. versions.tf**
```hcl
terraform {
  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
  required_providers {
    google = {
      source  = "hashicorp/google"
      version = ">= 5.0"
    }
  }
}
```

**2. variables.tf Pattern**
```hcl
variable "project_id" {
  description = "GCP project ID"
  type        = string
}

variable "environment" {
  description = "Environment (dev/qa/prod)"
  type        = string
  validation {
    condition     = contains(["dev", "qa", "prod"], var.environment)
    error_message = "Environment must be dev, qa, or prod"
  }
}

variable "enable_feature" {
  description = "Enable X feature"
  type        = bool
  default     = true
}

variable "config" {
  description = "Complex configuration"
  type = object({
    name  = string
    value = number
  })
  default = null
}
```

**3. outputs.tf Pattern**
```hcl
output "resource_name" {
  description = "Name of created resource"
  value       = google_resource.this.name
}

output "resource_id" {
  description = "ID of created resource"
  value       = google_resource.this.id
}
```

### Module Usage Pattern

```hcl
# Development (uses latest)
module "example" {
  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/MODULE_NAME?ref=main"
  
  project_id  = "my-project-dev"
  environment = "dev"
}

# Production (uses semantic version)
module "example" {
  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/MODULE_NAME?ref=v1.0.0"
  
  project_id  = "my-project-prod"
  environment = "prod"
}
```

---

## PART 5: SECURITY REQUIREMENTS (MANDATORY)

All modules enforce these organization policies:

### 1. Workload Identity (No Keys)
```hcl
# ✅ CORRECT
resource "google_service_account_iam_binding" "workload_identity" {
  service_account_id = google_service_account.sa.name
  role               = "roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser"
  members = ["serviceAccount:${var.project_id}.svc.id.goog[${var.namespace}/${var.ksa_name}]"]
}

# ❌ NEVER
resource "google_service_account_key" "bad" { }
```

### 2. Private Networking (No Public IPs)
```hcl
# ✅ CORRECT - Private endpoint
resource "google_sql_database_instance" "main" {
  settings {
    ip_configuration {
      ipv4_enabled    = false
      private_network = var.vpc_self_link
    }
  }
}
```

### 3. EU Data Residency Only
```hcl
# ✅ CORRECT
variable "region" {
  type = string
  validation {
    condition     = can(regex("^europe-", var.region))
    error_message = "Region must be in Europe (europe-west1 or europe-west4)"
  }
}
```

### 4. IAM Least Privilege
- Use predefined roles (no `roles/owner` or `roles/editor`)
- Grant minimum permissions required
- Use IAM conditions to restrict scope

### 5. GCP Health Check IPs Required for Network Policies
```json
{
  "allowed_ingress": [
    {"cidr_block": "35.191.0.0/16", "ports": [{"port": 8080, "protocol": "TCP"}]},
    {"cidr_block": "130.211.0.0/22", "ports": [{"port": 8080, "protocol": "TCP"}]}
  ]
}
```

## PART 6: COMPLETE MODULE CATALOG

### GCP Modules Location Map

**Infrastructure Foundation:**
- `terraform/gcp/base-environment/` - Folder + projects
- `terraform/gcp/landing-zone/` - VPC, NAT, firewall, DNS
- `terraform/gcp/public-dns/` - DNS zones
- `terraform/gcp/ssl-certificates/` - Managed SSL certs

**Compute & Orchestration:**
- `terraform/gcp/gke-autopilot/` - GKE cluster
- `terraform/gcp/gke-cluster/` - GKE Standard
- `terraform/kubernetes/gke-config/` - Namespaces, RBAC, network policies

**Data Services:**
- `terraform/gcp/data-services/cloud-sql/` - PostgreSQL instances
- `terraform/gcp/data-services/cloud-sql-db/` - Databases/users
- `terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs/` - Storage buckets
- `terraform/gcp/data-services/pubsub/` - Message queues
- `terraform/gcp/data-services/redis/` - In-memory cache

**Security & IAM:**
- `terraform/gcp/service-accounts/` - Service accounts + Workload Identity
- `terraform/gcp/secret-manager/` - Secrets with rotation
- `terraform/gcp/workload-identity/` - K8s-to-GCP federation
- `terraform/gcp/cloud-armor/` - WAF policies
- `terraform/gcp/github-actions-oidc/` - OIDC for CI/CD

**Platform Services:**
- `terraform/kubernetes/argocd/` - GitOps
- `terraform/kubernetes/kong-gateway/` - API gateway
- `terraform/kubernetes/litellm/` - LLM proxy
- `terraform/kubernetes/litellm-config/` - LLM configuration
- `terraform/kubernetes/otel-collector/` - Observability

## PART 7: WORKLOAD REPO NAMING & ORGANIZATION

### Workload Repo Naming Convention
```
konecta-ix-workloads/
├── kix-workload-mcp-gateway        # MCP Gateway (P1)
├── kix-workload-mcp-ragengine      # RAG Engine (P2)
├── kix-workload-iqportal           # IQ Portal (P0 pilot)
├── kix-workload-hrfraud            # HR Fraud (P3)
├── kix-workload-rag-ingestion      # RAG Ingestion (P4)
└── ...
```

### Kubernetes Namespace Pattern
```
{environment}-{service-name}

Examples:
- dev-mcp-gateway
- qa-mcp-gateway
- prod-mcp-gateway
- dev-iqportal
- qa-iqportal
- prod-iqportal
```

### Environment → Branch Mapping
```
develop branch → dev-{service} namespace
main branch    → qa-{service} namespace
v*.* tags      → prod-{service} namespace
```

## PART 8: COMPLETE SERVICE ONBOARDING PATH

### From Template to Deployment

1. **Create Code Repo** (from `kix-platform-service-template`)
   ```bash
   gh repo create konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-newservice \
     --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template
   ```

2. **Create Workload Repo** (from `kix-platform-workload-template`)
   ```bash
   gh repo create konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-newservice \
     --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-workload-template
   ```

3. **Configure Workload Repo** - Populate:
   - `helm/values-{env}.yaml` - Environment config
   - `argocd/application.yaml` - Git source, namespace, sync policy
   - `terraform/` - Service accounts, IAM, secrets
   - `.github/workflows/deploy.yaml` - Automation

4. **Register with ArgoCD**
   - Push `argocd/application.yaml`
   - ArgoCD controller picks it up
   - Helm charts deployed to cluster

5. **Register with Backstage**
   - Add `catalog-info.yaml` to code repo
   - Backstage discovers within 30 minutes
   - Shows in service catalog

## PART 10: CRITICAL IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

### Helm Values Pattern
```yaml
# values.yaml (defaults)
replicaCount: 2
image:
  repository: gcr.io/project/service
  tag: "latest"
resources:
  requests:
    cpu: 250m
    memory: 512Mi

# values-dev.yaml (dev overrides)
replicaCount: 1
image:
  tag: "develop"

# values-prod.yaml (prod overrides)
replicaCount: 3
image:
  tag: "v1.0.0"
resources:
  requests:
    cpu: 500m
    memory: 1Gi
```

### ArgoCD Application Pattern
```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: kix-mcp-gateway-dev
  namespace: shared-argocd
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-mcp-gateway
    targetRevision: develop
    path: helm
    helm:
      valuesFiles:
        - values.yaml
        - values-dev.yaml
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: dev-mcp-gateway
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
```

### Terraform Service Account Pattern
```hcl
module "service_accounts" {
  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/service-accounts?ref=v1.0.0"

  project_id              = "kd-ix-eur-dev-gke"
  environment             = "dev"
  application_name        = "mcp-gateway"
  namespace_prefix        = "mcp-gateway"
  platform_data_project   = "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform"
  existing_instance_name  = "mcp-gateway-db"

  secret_ids = [
    "mcp-gateway-dev-database-credentials",
    "mcp-gateway-dev-oauth-credentials",
  ]

  data_bucket_name = "mcp-gateway-dev-data"
  logs_bucket_name = "mcp-gateway-dev-logs"

  labels = {
    environment = "dev"
    application = "mcp-gateway"
    managed_by  = "terraform"
  }
}
```

**`README.md`** - Project overview describing Backstage as KIX Platform's internal developer portal (planning phase)

**`CLAUDE.md`** - Comprehensive operational guide with:
- Quick start instructions
