---

### GITHUB ACTIONS WORKFLOWS

**`.github/workflows/validate-and-plan.yml`**
- Auto-triggers on PR to main/develop for *.tf files
- Detects changed Terraform directories
- Runs validate and plan with Trivy security scanning
- Generates summary table of changes
- No apply, plan-only for review before merge

**`.github/workflows/validate-plan-apply.yml`**
- Manual workflow dispatch with environment selector (dev/qa/shared/prod)
- Branch-environment access control:
  - `main` → dev, qa, shared only
  - `develop` → dev only
  - `v*` version tags → prod only
- Production change window: Tuesday-Thursday, 20:00-23:00 UTC
- Manual approval gate before apply
- Uses reusable workflows from `.github-private`

**`.github/workflows/argocd-deploy.yml`**
- Manual deployment of ArgoCD Applications
- Branch-environment access control matching apply workflow
- Deploys from `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml`
- Requires `ARGOCD_AUTH_TOKEN` secret

**`.github/workflows/manual-validate-plan.yml`**
- Manual validate and plan without apply
- Same access control as apply workflow
- Useful for testing before deployment

---

### CONFIGURATION (config.json per environment)

All three environments (dev, qa, shared) follow identical structure with environment-specific values:

**Key Sections in config.json:**

1. **global** - Infrastructure references
   - environment, region, domain_base
   - project_ids (gke, security, network, data, ai)
   - gke_cluster (name, endpoint)
   - network CIDRs (service, master, cloudsql_psa)
   - cloud_sql (instance name, connection string, proxy image)
   - artifacts registry details
   - waf_policy, iap oauth config
   - labels

2. **service** - Backstage-specific config
   - name, namespace
   - gcp_service_account details
   - k8s_service_account name
   - hostname
   - certificate names and maps
   - database (name, user_iam)
   - secrets references
   - image (name, tag)
   - serviceAccount create flag
   - gateway enabled
   - backstage_config (title, catalog_rules, locations)
   - integrations (GitHub app env var names)
   - auth (guest mode config)

**Example Values:**
- **dev**: `backstage.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com`, cluster endpoint 35.241.228.46, db project `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data`
- **qa**: `backstage.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com`, cluster endpoint 34.52.156.174, db project `kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data`
- **shared**: `backstage.ix.konecta-digital.com`, PLACEHOLDER cluster endpoint, db project `kd-ix-eur-shr-platform-data`

---

### TERRAFORM FILES (6 files per environment × 3 environments = 18 files)

All environments follow identical patterns with values sourced from config.json via `locals.tf`.

**`versions.tf`** - Standard across all environments
```hcl
terraform {
  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
  required_providers {
    google = "~> 5.0"
    kubernetes = "~> 2.23"
    random = "~> 3.5"
  }
}
```

**`backend.tf`** - GCS backend per environment
```hcl
backend "gcs" {
  bucket = "kd-tfstate-services-{env}"
  prefix = "services/backstage/resources"
}
```

**`locals.tf`** - Same across all environments (loads config.json)
```hcl
locals {
  config  = jsondecode(file("${path.module}/../config.json"))
  global  = local.config.global
  service = local.config.service
}
```

**`variables.tf`**
```hcl
variable "run_database_init_job" {
  description = "Whether to run the database initialization job"
  type        = bool
  default     = true
}

variable "database_init_trigger" {
  description = "Change to re-run the init job (e.g. v1, v2)"
  type        = string
  default     = "v5"
}
```

**`main.tf`** - 517 lines of comprehensive resource definitions (identical across all environments):

1. **Providers** - Google, Kubernetes, configured with cluster data sources
2. **Namespace** - `kubernetes_namespace_v1` with labels
3. **GCP Service Account** - `google_service_account` "backstage"
4. **IAM Roles** - Cloud SQL client and instance user roles
5. **Kubernetes Service Account** - With Workload Identity annotation
6. **Workload Identity Binding** - `google_service_account_iam_binding`
7. **Backend Secret** - Random password generated and stored in GCP Secret Manager
8. **ExternalSecrets** - Three ExternalSecret resources for:
   - IAP OAuth (synced from GCP SM)
   - Backend secret (synced from GCP SM)
   - GitHub App credentials (synced from GCP SM)
9. **Database Initialization Job** - `kubernetes_job_v1` that:
   - Runs PostgreSQL client container
   - Uses Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar
   - Grants database privileges to IAM user
   - Conditionally triggered via `run_database_init_job` variable
10. **Certificate Manager Resources** (Service-Owned Pattern):
    - `google_certificate_manager_certificate` - Google-managed SSL
    - `google_certificate_manager_certificate_map` - Certificate holder
    - `google_certificate_manager_certificate_map_entry` - Hostname mapping
11. **Cloud SQL Database** - `google_sql_database` (service-owned on shared instance)
12. **Cloud SQL IAM User** - `google_sql_user` for IAM authentication

**`outputs.tf`** - Exports:
- ExternalSecret names
- Workload Identity binding info
- Backstage namespace name
- Environment name
- Database details (name, IAM user, connection string)
- Certificate map information (name, ID, hostname)

---

### HELM CHART STRUCTURE (Identical across all environments)

**`Chart.yaml`**
```yaml
apiVersion: v2
name: backstage
description: Backstage Developer Portal for KIX Platform
type: application
version: 1.0.0
appVersion: "1.47.3"
keywords:
  - backstage
  - developer-portal
  - service-catalog
  - platform
maintainers:
  - name: KIX Platform Team
    email: platform-team@konecta.com
home: https://docs.kix-platform.konecta.com/services/backstage
sources:
  - https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-backstage
```

**`values.yaml`** - Base values (280+ lines, no environment-specific overrides)
Sections:
- image (repository, tag, pullPolicy)
- replicaCount, service config
- serviceAccount with Workload Identity
- database config (Cloud SQL, proxy settings)
- backstage config (empty, filled by environment)
- secrets (backendSecret, githubApp)
- resource limits (250m/512Mi requests, 1000m/1Gi limits)
- autoscaling (disabled, 2-5 replicas when enabled)
- podDisruptionBudget (disabled)
- network policies (comprehensive config with 10 policy types)
- commonLabels (app, platform, managed-by)
- pod annotations and labels (compute class: Balanced)
- security contexts (runAsNonRoot, runAsUser: 1000)
- probes (liveness, readiness, startup)
- gateway config (disabled by default, hostname/certificateMap empty)
- IAP config (disabled by default)
- securityPolicy (empty)

**`values-{env}.yaml`** - AUTO-GENERATED per environment from config.json
Regenerated by `./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh {env}`

Contains:
- Image repository from Artifact Registry with version tag
- Service account Workload Identity annotation
- Database user (IAM format) and Cloud SQL Proxy connection string
- Backstage app-config.yaml with environment-specific values:
  - title, baseUrl, catalog locations
  - database connection (host, port, user, database)
  - auth providers (guest mode)
  - integrations (GitHub App with env var substitution)
- GitHub App secret references (ESO-synced)
- Gateway enabled with hostname and certificate map
- IAP enabled with OAuth client ID and secret name
- Security policy (WAF)
- Network policies with custom egress rules:
  - GCE metadata server (169.254.169.254)
  - Node-local DNS (169.254.20.10)
  - K8s API (service_cidr, master_cidr)
  - Cloud SQL PSA CIDR
- Environment labels (cost-center, managed-by)

---

### HELM TEMPLATES (13 files, identical across environments)

**`_helpers.tpl`** - Helm helper functions
- backstage.name - Chart name (Backstage)
- backstage.fullname - Full name
- backstage.chart - Chart label
- backstage.labels - Common labels
- backstage.selectorLabels - App selector
- backstage.serviceAccountName - SA name logic

**`deployment.yaml`** - Main Backstage deployment
- Replicas controlled by autoscaling if enabled
- Backstage container:
  - Image from registry
  - Command: `node packages/backend --config /app/app-config.yaml`
  - Security context (non-root, no escalation)
  - Ports: 7007 (http)
  - Env vars: BACKEND_SECRET, POSTGRES_USER, GITHUB_APP_* (from secrets)
  - Volume mounts: app-config ConfigMap, /tmp emptyDir
  - Probes: liveness, readiness, startup
  - Resource limits
- Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar (optional):
  - Args: --private-ip, --structured-logs, --auto-iam-authn
  - Security context (non-root, read-only filesystem)
- Pod security context (runAsUser 1000, seccomp)
- Node selector, affinity, tolerations (arch: amd64)

**`service.yaml`** - ClusterIP Service
- Type: ClusterIP
- Port: 80 → 7007 (containerPort)
- Standard metadata labels

**`serviceaccount.yaml`** - Kubernetes ServiceAccount
- Conditional creation (if serviceAccount.create=true)
- Workload Identity annotation (GCP SA email)

**`configmap.yaml`** - ConfigMap for app-config.yaml
- Renders `backstage.config` from values as YAML

**`gateway.yaml`** - GKE Gateway (conditional on gateway.enabled)
- Listeners: HTTP (80), HTTPS (443, optional)
- gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed
- Certificate map annotation: `networking.gke.io/certmap`
- NO TLS block (handled by Certificate Manager)

**`httproute.yaml`** - HTTPRoute for HTTPS traffic
- Parent: Gateway (https section)
- Hostname: from values
- Matches: PathPrefix "/"
- Backend: Service (port 80)

**`httproute-redirect.yaml`** - HTTPRoute for HTTP→HTTPS redirect
- Parent: Gateway (http section)
- Hostname: same
- Filter: RequestRedirect (scheme: https, status: 301)

**`networkpolicy.yaml`** - 10 NetworkPolicy resources (comprehensive)
1. Deny all ingress (default)
2. Allow GCP health checks (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
3. Allow from system namespace (kube-system)
4. Allow GMP collectors (gke-gmp-system, conditional)
5. Custom ingress rules (environment-specific)
6. Allow DNS egress (UDP/TCP 53 to kube-system)
7. Allow Google APIs egress (TCP 443 to specific CIDRs)
8. Allow internet egress (conditional, 0.0.0.0/0 except private ranges)
9. Custom egress rules (environment-specific)
10. Allow same-namespace intra-pod communication

**`gcpbackendpolicy.yaml`** - GCPBackendPolicy (conditional on gateway.enabled && iap.enabled)
- Default scope:
  - IAP: enabled, OAuth client secret from K8s, client ID
  - Security policy (Cloud Armor WAF)
  - Logging: enabled, sampleRate 1.0
  - Timeout: 30 seconds
- Target: Service (Backstage service)

**`healthcheckpolicy.yaml`** - HealthCheckPolicy (conditional on gateway.enabled)
- Type: HTTP
- Path: /healthcheck
- Port: 7007
- Intervals and thresholds configurable

**`pdb.yaml`** - PodDisruptionBudget (conditional on podDisruptionBudget.enabled)
- minAvailable: configurable
- Selector: backstage pods

**`hpa.yaml`** - HorizontalPodAutoscaler (conditional on autoscaling.enabled)
- Target: Backstage Deployment
- Min/Max replicas: 2-5
- Metrics: CPU (70%), Memory (80%)
- Behavior: scale up 100%/60s, scale down 10%/60s

**`NOTES.txt`** - Post-deployment instructions
- Shows resources deployed
- Port-forward command for local access
- Health check test command
- Next steps (verify pods, logs, network policies)

---

### ARGOCD APPLICATION MANIFESTS (3 files, one per environment)

All follow identical pattern:

**Structure:**
- apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
- kind: Application
- metadata: name (backstage-{env}), namespace: argocd, labels, finalizer
- spec:
  - project: platform
  - source:
    - repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-backstage
    - targetRevision: main
    - path: environments/{env}/helm/backstage
    - helm:
      - valueFiles: [values.yaml, values-{env}.yaml]
      - skipCrds: false
  - destination:
    - server: Environment-specific cluster URL
    - namespace: backstage
  - syncPolicy:
    - automated: prune, selfHeal, allowEmpty: false
    - retry: 5 attempts, exponential backoff
    - syncOptions: CreateNamespace, Validate, RespectIgnoreDifferences
  - ignoreDifferences: 12 patterns for:
    - Deployment replicas (HPA)
    - Service annotations (GKE NEG)
    - Config checksum (changes per sync)
    - Gateway/HTTPRoute/GCPBackendPolicy/HealthCheckPolicy status

**Environment Differences:**
- **dev**: server: https://35.241.228.46
- **qa**: server: https://34.52.156.174
- **shared**: server: https://kubernetes.default.svc (in-cluster)

### SCRIPTS

**`scripts/generate-helm-values.sh`** - 296 lines
- Bash script that generates values-{env}.yaml from config.json
- Supports `--check` mode to verify generated file matches config
- Used in CI to validate values files aren't manually edited
- Extractsfrom config.json:
  - Environment, project IDs, network CIDRs
  - Hostname, certificate map, gateway enabled
  - Database details, image info
  - Auth config, GitHub integration
  - Catalog locations
- Generates complete values-{env}.yaml with:
  - Image repository (Artifact Registry path)
  - Service account annotation
  - Database configuration
  - app-config.yaml content
  - GitHub App secrets
  - Gateway and IAP settings
  - Network policy egress rules
  - Environment labels
- Usage:
  - `./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh dev` - Generate
  - `./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh dev --check` - Verify (CI mode)

### DOCUMENTATION

**`docs/backstage-deployment-plan.md`** - 347 lines
- Executive summary: Deploy per-environment Backstage instances
- Architecture: Dev/QA/Prod clusters have their own Backstage, managed by shared ArgoCD
- Per-environment topology diagram
- Technical design:
  - Helm chart strategy (official backstage/backstage chart)
  - Database: Cloud SQL with IAM auth (same pattern as LiteLLM)
  - Service discovery: Hybrid (static catalog + dynamic K8s plugin)
  - Authentication Phase 1: IAP (MVP)
  - Authentication Phase 2: External IdP (future, optional)
- Implementation approach (5 phases):
  - Core infrastructure (Terraform module, dev first)
  - Gateway & authentication
  - Configuration repository (kon-backstage-config)
  - ArgoCD integration
  - Catalog bootstrap
- Security: Network policies, secrets management (IAM auth, ESO)
- Cost estimate: ~$270/month all environments
- Rollout strategy: Dev → QA → Prod
- Success criteria per environment
- Future enhancements
- References
- Decision log with rationale

---

## KEY PATTERNS & CONVENTIONS

### 1. **Configuration-Driven Deployment**
- Single `config.json` per environment contains all values
- Terraform `locals.tf` loads config via `jsondecode(file())`
- `generate-helm-values.sh` creates Helm values from config
- CI validates Helm values match config (no manual edits)

### 2. **Service-Owned Resources**
- Backstage owns its database (service-owned pattern)
- Creates database on shared Cloud SQL instance
- Owns certificate and certificate map
- IAM user created by Terraform for IAM auth

### 3. **Workload Identity**
- GCP service account per environment
- K8s service account with Workload Identity annotation
- Cloud SQL Proxy uses automatic IAM authentication
- No password management

### 4. **External Secrets Operator**
- Terraform creates ExternalSecret manifests
- Three secrets synced from GCP Secret Manager:
  - IAP OAuth secret
  - Backend secret (random generated)
  - GitHub App credentials
- ESO controller syncs to K8s secrets

### 5. **Multi-Environment Support**
- Identical Terraform code across dev/qa/shared
- Configuration via external config.json
- Same Helm chart, different values per environment
- All ArgoCD Applications from same repository

### 6. **Network Security**
- Default deny all ingress
- Allow specific health check IPs
- Allow DNS, Google APIs, Cloud SQL
- Custom egress rules per environment
- Intra-namespace pod communication allowed

### 7. **Gateway & Certificate Management**
- GKE Gateway API (not Ingress)
- Certificate Manager handles TLS (no inline certs)
- HTTP redirects to HTTPS (RequestRedirect filter)
- Annotation-based certificate map reference

### 8. **IAP for Access Control**
- GCPBackendPolicy with IAP enabled
- No application-level auth complexity
- Can migrate to external IdP later without infrastructure changes

### 9. **Helm Values Generation**
- Base values.yaml contains all defaults
- Environment values-{env}.yaml auto-generated
- Direct editing of values-{env}.yaml forbidden
- CI enforces this with `--check` mode

### 10. **Database Initialization**
- Optional Kubernetes Job runs on demand
- Grants database privileges to IAM user
- Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar pattern
- Conditional execution via variable

---

## NAMING CONVENTIONS

**GCP Resources:**
- Pattern: `kd-ix-eur-{env}-{resource}-{service}`
- Example: `kd-ix-eur-dev-cert-backstage`, `kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform`

**Kubernetes:**
- Namespace: `backstage`
- Service account: `backstage-sa`
- Helm release: `backstage` (via ArgoCD)
- All named resources use Helm helpers (backstage.fullname)

**Secrets (GCP Secret Manager):**
- `{env}-gateway-iap-oauth-secret`
- `backstage-backend-secret`
- `backstage-github-app-{id|private-key|client-id|client-secret}`
- `cloudsql-postgres-password`

**Hostnames:**
- Dev: `backstage.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com`
- QA: `backstage.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com`
- Shared: `backstage.ix.konecta-digital.com`

---

## CRITICAL OPERATIONAL NOTES

1. **Never manually edit `values-{env}.yaml`** - Run `./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh {env}` after config changes
2. **Database init job trigger** - Change `database_init_trigger` variable to re-run (v1 → v2)
3. **Workload Identity binding** - Allows K8s SA to assume GCP SA identity
4. **Cloud SQL Proxy**- Runs as sidecar, required for IAM auth
5. **Certificate ownership** - Service owns certs (vs centralized in infrastructure repo)
6. **ArgoCD ignoreDifferences** - Prevents drift from GKE-managed fields
7. **Change windows** - Production deployments: Tue-Thu, 20:00-23:00 UTC only

This is a production-ready, enterprise-grade configuration for deploying Backstage across multiple GCP-hosted Kubernetes clusters with full IaC, GitOps, security, and operational patterns established.

  "content": "# Feature Brainstorming Session: IQ Portal Workload Infrastructure\n\n**Date:** 2026-02-16\n**Session Type:** Technical Design / Infrastructure Planning\n\n## 1. Context & Problem Statement\n\n### Problem Description\nThe KonectaIQ Portal application currently runs on Google Cloud Run and needs to be migrated to the kix-infrastructure GKE platform. The workload repository (`kix-workload-konectaiq`) must contain all infrastructure-as-code (Terraform, Helm, ArgoCD, CI/CD) required to deploy and operate the application on GKE across dev, QA, and prod environments.\n\n### Target Users\n- **Primary Users:** Platform Engineering team deploying and managing the IQ Portal on GKE\n- **Secondary Users:** IQ Portal development team consuming the infrastructure, SRE/ops teams monitoring the service\n\n### Success Criteria\n- **Business Metrics:** Zero-downtime migration from Cloud Run to GKE; all 92 GitHub issues tracked and closed as work completes\n- **Technical Metrics:** All three environments (dev/qa/prod) deployable via ArgoCD; Terraform plans clean; Helm charts lint and template successfully\n- **User Metrics:** Developer experience matches or exceeds current Cloud Run workflow (push-to-deploy via GitOps)\n\n### Constraints & Assumptions\n- **Technical Constraints:**\n  - Must follow the established pattern from `kix-services-backstage` (environment-based directory structure, config.json-driven, generate-helm-values.sh)\n  - Cloud SQL instances already exist in `kd-ix-eur-<env>-platform-data` projects — only database creation and IAM needed\n  - Must use approved Cloud SQL Auth Proxy image from Artifact Registry\n  - Must use GKE Gateway API + IAP for frontend ingress (browser users)\n  - Must use Kong for API ingress (machine-to-machine consumers)\n  - EU data residency enforced (europe-west1 / europe-west4 only)\n- **Business Constraints:** P0 pilot — this is the first full workload migration and will validate the entire platform\n- **Regulatory/Compliance:** No service account keys (Workload Identity only); Pod Security Standards restricted profile; signed container images in prod\n- **Assumptions Made:**\n  - Shared Kong gateway is operational in `shared-kong` namespace\n  - GKE Gateway API + IAP is configured on the cluster\n  - ESO (External Secrets Operator) is running in the cluster\n  - ArgoCD is running in `shared-argocd` namespace (or `argocd` — to verify)\n  - Platform modules in `kix-platform-modules` are stable and usable via Git source references\n\n## 2. Brainstormed Ideas & Options\n\n### Option A: Follow Migration Guide Structure (Flat Layout)\n- **Description:** Use the flat directory structure from the iq-portal.md migration guide: top-level `helm/`, `argocd/`, `terraform/` directories with per-environment values files.\n- **Key Features:**\n  - `helm/values.yaml`, `helm/values-dev.yaml`, `helm/values-qa.yaml`, `helm/values-prod.yaml`\n  - Single `terraform/main.tf` with environment passed as variable\n  - Single `argocd/application.yaml`\n- **Pros:**\n  - Simpler initial structure\n  - Matches the migration guide documentation\n- **Cons:**\n  - Does NOT match the established reference repo (`kix-services-backstage`)\n  - No config.json pattern — values scattered and harder to maintain\n  - Terraform state management across environments is unclear\n  - Will require rework to align with team standards\n- **Effort Estimate:** M\n- **Risk Level:** High (diverges from proven pattern)\n- **Dependencies:** Migration guide only\n\n### Option B: Follow Backstage Reference Pattern (Environment-Based Layout)\n- **Description:** Replicate the `kix-services-backstage` structure exactly: `environments/{env}/` directories each containing `argocd/`, `helm/`, `terraform/`, and `config.json`. Include `scripts/generate-helm-values.sh` for consistency.\n- **Key Features:**\n  - `environments/dev/`, `environments/qa/`, `environments/prod/` directories\n  - `config.json` per environment driving all configuration\n  - Identical Terraform code per environment, differentiated by config.json\n  - Identical Helm templates per environment, values generated from config.json\n  - Same GitHub Actions workflows (validate-and-plan, validate-plan-apply, argocd-deploy, manual-validate-plan)\n- **Pros:**\n  - Matches the proven, production-validated pattern\n  - Config.json provides single source of truth per environment\n  - CI can validate generated values match config (--check mode)\n  - Team already familiar with this layout\n  - Consistent across all workload repos\n- **Cons:**\n  - Helm templates duplicated across environments (boilerplate)\n  - More files to maintain (though config.json reduces drift risk)\n  - Migration guide needs updating to reflect this pattern\n- **Effort Estimate:** L\n- **Risk Level:** Low (proven pattern)\n- **Dependencies:** kix-services-backstage as reference\n\n### Option C: Hybrid — Shared Templates with Per-Environment Config\n- **Description:** Use environment directories for config.json, terraform, and argocd, but share Helm templates via a single `helm/` directory with environment-specific values generated into `environments/{env}/helm/values-{env}.yaml`.\n- **Key Features:**\n  - Single `helm/templates/` directory (no duplication)\n  - `environments/{env}/config.json` and `environments/{env}/terraform/` per environment\n  - ArgoCD points to shared helm path with environment-specific values overlay\n- **Pros:**\n  - Reduces template duplication\n  - Still uses config.json pattern\n- **Cons:**\n  - Deviates from the reference pattern\n  - ArgoCD multi-source configuration is more complex\n  - No precedent in the organization\n- **Effort Estimate:** M\n- **Risk Level:** Medium (untested pattern)\n- **Dependencies:** Custom ArgoCD configuration\n\n### Additional Ideas Considered\n- Using Kustomize instead of Helm — rejected, Helm is the established standard\n- Using Helmfile for multi-environment orchestration — adds unnecessary tooling\n- Terraform workspaces instead of per-environment directories — doesn't match reference pattern\n\n## 3. Decision Outcome\n\n### Chosen Approach\n**Selected Solution:** Option B — Follow Backstage Reference Pattern (Environment-Based Layout)\n\n### Rationale\n**Primary Factors in Decision:**\n- **Consistency:** The Backstage repo is explicitly provided as \"what good looks like\" — deviating from it creates organizational inconsistency\n- **Proven:** The pattern is already in production, validated by the platform team\n- **Maintainability:** Config.json as single source of truth with generated values reduces configuration drift\n- **Team Familiarity:** Engineers already know this layout from the Backstage deployment\n\n### Trade-offs Accepted\n- **What We're Gaining:** Organizational consistency, proven patterns, CI validation of config, single source of truth per environment\n- **What We're Sacrificing:** Helm template duplication across environments (minor — templates rarely change independently)\n- **Future Considerations:** The migration guide documentation should be updated to reflect this pattern as the standard\n\n## 4. Risks & Dependencies\n\n### Technical Risks\n\n- **Risk:** Config.json values may have incorrect GCP project IDs or resource references\n  - **Impact:** High\n  - **Probability:** Medium\n  - **Mitigation Strategy:** Terraform plan will catch most misconfigurations; start with dev environment only\n\n- **Risk:** IQ Portal has more complex ingress requirements than Backstage (dual: Kong API + Gateway frontend)\n  - **Impact:** Medium\n  - **Probability:** Low\n  - **Mitigation Strategy:** Implement Gateway API for frontend first (matching Backstage pattern), add Kong route configuration in Terraform\n\n- **Risk:** ExternalSecrets reference names may not match what ESO expects in the cluster\n  - **Impact:** High\n  - **Probability:** Medium\n  - **Mitigation Strategy:** Verify SecretStore names and GCP Secret Manager paths during terraform plan; align with platform team\n\n- **Risk:** Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar configuration differs between Backstage (IAM auth) and IQ Portal (password auth)\n  - **Impact:** Medium\n  - **Probability:** High\n  - **Mitigation Strategy:** IQ Portal currently uses password-based auth; Terraform will create the database user and password, Cloud SQL proxy provides connectivity but auth uses DB_PASSWORD from ExternalSecret\n\n## 7. Session Notes & Insights\n\n### Key Insights Discovered\n\n- **The migration guide structure does NOT match the reference repo.** The guide shows a flat `helm/`, `terraform/`, `argocd/` layout, but the established pattern from `kix-services-backstage` uses `environments/{env}/` directories with `config.json` driving everything. The reference repo pattern must take precedence.\n\n- **IQ Portal uses password-based DB auth, not IAM auth.** Backstage uses Cloud SQL IAM authentication (no passwords), but IQ Portal uses `DB_PASSWORD` with traditional PostgreSQL credentials. The Terraform needs to create a standard database user with a random password (stored in Secret Manager), not an IAM user.\n\n- **More secrets than Backstage.** IQ Portal has ~12 secret values vs Backstage's ~4. The ExternalSecret configuration is more complex, mapping: DB_PASSWORD, DB_USER, JWT_SECRET, GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID, VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID, RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN, CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID, CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET, CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID, GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET, GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET, GCS_RAG_BUCKET.\n\n- **Dual ingress requirement.** Backstage only uses Gateway API. IQ Portal needs both Gateway API (frontend browser access with IAP) and Kong (API consumers at `/api/iq-portal`). This adds Kong route/service Terraform resources and possibly a KongIngress Helm template.\n\n- **The app serves both frontend and API on the same port (8080).** In the Docker container, Express serves the Nuxt SPA from `/dist` and API from `/api/*` on port 8080. This means a single Service with two ingress paths, not two separate services.\n\n### Documentation Updates Required\nThe following corrections need to be made to `kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/service-guides/iq-portal.md`:\n\n1. **GCP project mapping:** Vertex AI uses `kd-ix-eur-<env>-platform-ai`, GCS and Cloud Tasks use `kd-ix-eur-<env>-platform-data` (not generic placeholders)\n2. **Cloud SQL instance connection names:** `kd-ix-eur-<env>-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-<env>-cloudsql-platform`\n3. **Database name:** `kix-iqportal` (new convention, not `iqplatform`)\n4. **GCS bucket naming:** `kix-iqportal-images-<env>`, `kix-iqportal-videos-<env>`, `kix-iqportal-rag-<env>`\n5. **Service account project:** `kd-ix-eur-<env>-gke` (not `kd-ix-platform`)\n6. **Cloud SQL Auth Proxy image:** `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-shr-artifacts/approved-images/cloud-sql-proxy` (not public GCR)\n7. **Workload repo structure:** Should reference `kix-services-backstage` environment-based pattern, not the flat structure currently shown\n8. **Terraform state bucket:** `kd-tfstate-services-<env>/services/kix-iqportal` (not - **More GCP service dependencies** — Vertex AI, GCS, Cloud Tasks in addition to Cloud SQL

---

## Phase 1 Surface Discovery Results

### Project Context Summary

**Current repo state:** The `kix-workload-konectaiq` repo (`/home/nathan_booth/code/kix-workload-konectaiq/`) is essentially empty -- it contains only a `README.md` and the `docs/` directory with the brainstorming document and templates. All infrastructure code needs to be generated from scratch.

**Reference repo (`/tmp/kix-services-backstage/`):** Fully built out with:
- 4 environments (`dev`, `qa`, `shared`) -- note: no `prod` directory exists yet in reference
- `environments/{env}/config.json` driving all configuration via `locals.tf`
- Terraform: namespace, GCP SA, Workload Identity, Cloud SQL (IAM auth), ExternalSecrets, Certificate Manager, DB init job
- Helm chart with 13 template files: deployment, service, serviceaccount, hpa, pdb, networkpolicy, gateway, httproute, httproute-redirect, gcpbackendpolicy, healthcheckpolicy, configmap, NOTES.txt, _helpers.tpl
- 4 GitHub Actions workflows calling shared reusable workflows from `konecta-ix-services/.github-private`
- `scripts/generate-helm-values.sh` for config-driven values generation

**Application repo (`/tmp/kix-apps-konectaiq/`):** Nuxt/Express app with ~25 environment variables consumed from `backend/config/constants.ts`. Health endpoint at `/api/health`, runs on port 8080 (production), uses password-based DB auth.

### Task Completeness Assessment

**COMPLETE -- Areas with sufficient detail:**

1. **Repository structure:** The brainstorming document at `/home/nathan_booth/code/kix-workload-konectaiq/docs/brainstorming/2026-02-16-iqportal-workload-infrastructure.md` (lines 308-367) provides a complete target directory tree.

2. **GCP project mapping:** Full 3-environment reference table at lines 287-304 with project IDs, Cloud SQL connection strings, service accounts, hostnames, and bucket names for dev/qa/prod.

3. **Architectural decisions:** Option B (follow Backstage reference pattern) is clearly chosen and rationalized (lines 104-116).

4. **Terraform state backend:** Bucket names and prefixes resolved per environment (lines 292-293).

5. **Cloud SQL approach:** Password-based auth (not IAM auth) is explicitly called out as different from Backstage (lines 234-235, 195-196). Database name `kix-iqportal` confirmed.

6. **Secrets enumeration:** All 12 secret values listed (line 236): DB_PASSWORD, DB_USER, JWT_SECRET, GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID, VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID, RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN, CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID, CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET, CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID, GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET, GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET, GCS_RAG_BUCKET.

7. **Dual ingress requirement:** Gateway API for frontend + Kong for API is documented (lines 238-241).

8. **Health check path:** Application uses `/api/health` on port 8080 (confirmed from app source at `/tmp/kix-apps-konectaiq/backend/routes/index.ts`).

9. **GitHub issue mapping:** Detailed mapping of which issues correspond to which files (lines 256-283).

10. **CI/CD workflows:** All 4 workflow names specified, matching the reference repo pattern.

**GAPS IDENTIFIED -- Missing but addressable from reference patterns:**

1. **Network CIDR values for QA and prod environments.** The brainstorming document provides the full config reference table (lines 287-304) but does not include the `network` block values (`service_cidr`, `master_cidr`, `cloudsql_psa_cidr`) for QA and prod. The reference repo's dev config (`/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/config.json`, lines 18-21) shows these are required for network policy egress rules. The brainstorming doc only references dev values implicitly.

2. **GKE cluster names and endpoints for QA and prod.** The reference repo's `config.json` includes `gke_cluster.name` and `gke_cluster.endpoint` (used by the Terraform Kubernetes provider and ArgoCD application destination). The brainstorming doc does not provide QA/prod cluster endpoints.

3. **IAP OAuth2 client IDs per environment.** The reference repo shows an `iap.oauth2_client_id` in each `config.json`. The brainstorming document does not specify the IAP client IDs for dev/qa/prod IQ Portal deployments.

4. **WAF/Cloud Armor policy names per environment.** The reference has `waf_policy` in the global config. Not explicitly provided for IQ Portal environments.

5. **Certificate names and certificate map names per environment.** The brainstorming doc does not explicitly list certificate manager resource names, though these can be derived from the naming convention (`kd-ix-eur-{env}-cert-iqportal`, etc.).

6. **"Shared" environment.** The reference repo has a `shared` environment directory. The brainstorming doc only lists dev/qa/prod. It needs to be decided whether IQ Portal requires a shared environment or not.

7. **Additional environment variables not listed as secrets.** The app consumes many non-secret env vars that need to be set as plain environment variables in the deployment: `PORT`, `NODE_ENV`, `DB_HOST`, `DB_PORT`, `DB_NAME`, `GCP_PROJECT_ID`, `GCP_LOCATION`, `VERTEX_AI_PROJECT_ID`, `VERTEX_AI_LOCATION`, `VERTEX_AI_MODEL`, `RAG_PROJECT_ID`, `RAG_LOCATION`, `RAG_CORPUS_ID`, `CLOUD_TASKS_PROJECT_ID`, `CLOUD_TASKS_QUEUE`, `CLOUD_TASKS_LOCATION`, `CLOUD_TASKS_HANDLER_URL`, `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN`, `CHANNELHUB_URL`, `CHANNELHUB_LOGIN_URL`, `MAX_FILE_UPLOAD_SIZE_MB`, `NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT`, `MCP_GATEWAY_URL`. The brainstorming doc's secrets list (line 236) lists some GCS bucket values as secrets, but the non-secret env vars are not enumerated anywhere.

8. **IAM roles beyond Cloud SQL.** The app accesses Vertex AI, Cloud Storage (3 buckets), Cloud Tasks, and Secret Manager. The brainstorming doc mentions IAM roles at a high level (issue #6) but does not enumerate the specific IAM bindings needed: `roles/storage.objectAdmin` on data project, `roles/aiplatform.user` on AI project, `roles/cloudtasks.enqueuer` on data project, etc.

9. **Kong route configuration specifics.** The brainstorming doc mentions Kong for API ingress at `/api/iq-portal` (line 239) but does not detail the Kong service/route Terraform resources or whether this should be a KongIngress Helm template vs. Terraform-managed Kong configuration.

10. **Database user name.** For password-based auth, a SQL user needs to be created. The brainstorming doc lists `DB_USER` as a secret but does not specify the actual database username to create (e.g., `kix-iqportal` or `iqportal`).

**CRITICAL UNCERTAINTIES -- Items that could block correct implementation:**

None truly blocking. All gaps listed above can be reasonably inferred or use placeholder values that can be corrected during `terraform plan` validation. The brainstorming document is exceptionally thorough.

### Recommended User Clarifications

#### Priority 1: Values needed for config.json completeness

1. **Question:** What are the GKE cluster endpoints for QA and prod environments?
   - **Context:** The ArgoCD application manifest and Terraform Kubernetes provider need the cluster endpoint URL (e.g., `https://35.241.228.46` for dev as seen in the reference repo).
   - **Options:** A) Provide the endpoints now B) Use placeholder values (e.g., `PLACEHOLDER_CLUSTER_ENDPOINT`) and fill in during terraform plan
   - **Impact:** ArgoCD application manifests will not be deployable without real endpoints, but all other validation will still pass.

2. **Question:** Does IQ Portal need a "shared" environment like Backstage has?
   - **Context:** The reference repo at `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/shared/` has a full shared environment. The brainstorming doc only lists dev/qa/prod.
   - **Options:** A) No shared environment -- only dev/qa/prod B) Include shared environment with placeholder config
   - **Impact:** Affects directory structure and number of config files to generate.

3. **Question:** What are the network CIDR blocks for QA and prod clusters?
   - **Context:** Required for network policy egress rules (service_cidr, master_cidr, cloudsql_psa_cidr) -- see reference at `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/config.json` lines 18-21.
   - **Options:** A) Provide the CIDRs now B) Reuse dev CIDRs as placeholders C) Leave as TODO comments
   - **Impact:** Network policies will not correctly allow Cloud SQL and Kubernetes API access without correct CIDRs, but this can be corrected post-generation.

#### Priority 2: Integration and configuration details

4. **Question:** For the non-secret environment variables (VERTEX_AI_PROJECT_ID, RAG_PROJECT_ID, RAG_CORPUS_ID, CLOUD_TASKS_* vars, CHANNELHUB_URL, MCP_GATEWAY_URL, etc.), should these be:
   - **Options:** A) Hardcoded in the Helm values files per environment B) Managed via a ConfigMap with values driven from config.json C) Set as plain env vars in the deployment template with values from values.yaml
   - **Impact:** Determines whether config.json needs ~15 additional fields and whether the generate-helm-values.sh script needs significant expansion beyond the Backstage pattern.

5. **Question:** For the Kong API route, what is the expected path prefix and upstream service configuration?
   - **Context:** The brainstorming doc mentions `/api/iq-portal` at line 239. Should this be managed as Terraform resources (Kong service + route) or as Helm templates (KongIngress CRD)?
   - **Options:** A) Terraform-managed Kong resources (matching how the reference handles external integrations) B) Helm template with KongIngress CRD C) Defer Kong to Phase 2
   - **Impact:** Affects whether we add Kong-related Terraform resources and/or Helm templates now.

### Recommendation

- **DECISION:** PROCEED to Phase 2 (PRP generation)
- **REASONING:** The brainstorming document is exceptionally comprehensive. It resolves all major architectural decisions, provides concrete values for all three environments, maps GitHub issues to specific files, and explicitly documents where IQ Portal differs from the Backstage reference (password auth vs IAM auth, 12 secrets vs 4, dual ingress vs single). The gaps identified above are all either derivable from naming conventions, fillable with placeholders, or addressable during the terraform plan validation phase. None of them block the generation of structurally correct infrastructure code.
- **NEXT STEPS:**
  1. Generate the PRP document covering all deliverables listed in the brainstorming doc's MVP scope (lines 120-149)
  2. Use placeholder values for cluster endpoints, network CIDRs, and IAP client IDs where real values are unknown -- mark these clearly as `PLACEHOLDER_*`
  3. Skip the "shared" environment unless instructed otherwise (only generate dev/qa/prod)
  4. For non-secret env vars, follow the Backstage pattern of embedding them in the Helm values files driven by config.json
  5. Defer Kong route configuration to a dedicated Terraform resource block but include it in the initial implementation with clear documentation

### Codebase Context for Phase 2

**Reference files to study deeply during implementation:**

- **Terraform pattern:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` -- 517 lines covering namespace, SA, Workload Identity, Secret Manager, ExternalSecrets, Cloud SQL, Certificate Manager, DB init job
- **Config.json structure:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/config.json` -- 107 lines; IQ Portal version will need additional fields for secrets, Vertex AI, RAG, Cloud Tasks, ChannelHub
- **Helm values base:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/values.yaml` -- 214 lines; IQ Portal needs different env vars, port 8080, password-based DB auth
- **Deployment template:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/templates/deployment.yaml` -- 138 lines; needs adaptation for IQ Portal env vars and password-based proxy config (remove `--auto-iam-authn`)
- **Network policy template:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/templates/networkpolicy.yaml` -- 274 lines; reusable as-is with value changes
- **Generate script:** `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/scripts/generate-helm-values.sh` -- 297 lines; needs significant rewrite for IQ Portal config structure

## Comprehensive Codebase Analysis Report for IQ Portal Workload Infrastructure PRP

---

### 1. Project Overview

**Current state of `kix-workload-konectaiq`:** Nearly empty repository with only a `README.md` and a `docs/` directory containing a brainstorming session document, PRP/task templates, and no implementation code. The repo has a single initial commit on `main`.

**Target:** Build a complete workload infrastructure repository following the `kix-services-backstage` pattern to deploy the KonectaIQ Portal application (currently on Cloud Run) to the KIX GKE platform across dev, QA, and prod environments.

**Key Technologies and Frameworks:**
- **Terraform** >= 1.5.0 with Google provider ~> 5.0, Kubernetes provider ~> 2.23, Random provider ~> 3.5
- **Helm** v3.x charts (standalone, no base chart dependency)
- **ArgoCD** for GitOps deployment (single-source Git HTTPS)
- **GKE Gateway API** for frontend ingress (with IAP and Cloud Armor)
- **External Secrets Operator (ESO)** for secret synchronization from GCP Secret Manager
- **Cloud SQL Auth Proxy** sidecar for database connectivity
- **GitHub Actions** with reusable workflows from `konecta-ix-services/.github-private`
- **Trivy** for IaC security scanning

**Architecture Pattern:** Environment-based directory structure (`environments/{env}/`) with `config.json` as single source of truth per environment, auto-generated Helm values via `scripts/generate-helm-values.sh`.

---

### 2. Pattern Analysis

#### 2.1 Directory Structure Pattern (from kix-services-backstage)

The established pattern uses per-environment directories, each self-contained:

```
environments/{env}/
  config.json              # Single source of truth
  argocd/application.yaml  # ArgoCD Application manifest
  helm/{chart-name}/       # Full Helm chart
    Chart.yaml
    values.yaml            # Base defaults
    values-{env}.yaml      # AUTO-GENERATED from config.json
    templates/             # All K8s resource templates
  terraform/               # Environment-specific TF
    backend.tf
    locals.tf
    main.tf
    outputs.tf
    variables.tf
    versions.tf
```

File path: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/` (full tree shown above in structural analysis)

#### 2.2 Naming Conventions

From the brainstorming document at `/home/nathan_booth/code/kix-workload-konectaiq/docs/brainstorming/2026-02-16-iqportal-workload-infrastructure.md` (lines 286-304):

| Resource | Pattern |
|----------|---------|
| Helm chart name | `kix-app-iqportal` |
| K8s namespace | `{env}-iqportal` (e.g., `dev-iqportal`) |
| GCP Service Account | `kix-app-iqportal@kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com` |
| K8s Service Account | `kix-app-iqportal-sa` |
| Database name | `kix-iqportal` |
| TF state bucket | `kd-tfstate-services-{env}` |
| TF state prefix | `services/kix-iqportal/resources` |
| Frontend hostname | `iq-portal.{env}.ix.konecta-digital.com` (prod: `iq-portal.ix.konecta-digital.com`) |
| Certificate name | `kd-ix-eur-{env}-cert-iqportal` |
| GCS buckets | `kix-iqportal-images-{env}`, `kix-iqportal-videos-{env}`, `kix-iqportal-rag-{env}` |
| App image | `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-shr-artifacts/approved-images/kix-app-iqportal` |

#### 2.4 Terraform Pattern

From `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/locals.tf` (lines 1-5), configuration is loaded from `config.json`:
```hcl

  config  = jsondecode(file("${path.module}/../config.json"))
  global  = local.config.global
  service = local.config.service
}
```

From `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` (lines 1-517), the Terraform creates these resource types (in order):
1. Provider configuration (google + kubernetes)
2. Data sources (google_client_config, google_container_cluster)
3. Kubernetes namespace
4. GCP Service Account + IAM bindings
5. Kubernetes Service Account (with Workload Identity annotation)
6. Workload Identity binding
7. Secret Manager secrets + versions
8. Cloud SQL database + IAM user
9. ExternalSecret resources (kubernetes_manifest)
10. Database init job (optional)
11. Certificate Manager resources

#### 2.5 Helm Template Pattern

From the backstage templates at `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/templates/`:
- `_helpers.tpl` - Standard Helm helpers (name, fullname, chart, labels, selectorLabels, serviceAccountName)
- `deployment.yaml` - Main deployment with optional sidecar (Cloud SQL Proxy), configMap volume mounts, env vars from secrets
- `service.yaml` - ClusterIP service
- `serviceaccount.yaml` - Conditional ServiceAccount with Workload Identity annotation
- `hpa.yaml` - Conditional HPA with v2 API
- `pdb.yaml` - Conditional PDB
- `networkpolicy.yaml` - 10 network policies (deny-all, health-checks, system, GMP, custom-ingress, DNS, Google APIs, internet-egress, custom-egress, same-namespace)
- `gateway.yaml` - GKE Gateway with HTTP + HTTPS listeners
- `httproute.yaml` - HTTPS route to backend
- `httproute-redirect.yaml` - HTTP to HTTPS redirect
- `gcpbackendpolicy.yaml` - IAP + Cloud Armor + logging
- `healthcheckpolicy.yaml` - GKE health check configuration
- `configmap.yaml` - Application config rendered from values

#### 2.6 ArgoCD Pattern

From `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml` (lines 1-97):
- Project: `platform`
- Source: Git HTTPS single-source with Helm valueFiles
- Automated sync with prune, selfHeal
- ignoreDifferences for HPA replicas, GKE annotations, Gateway/HTTPRoute status, GCPBackendPolicy/HealthCheckPolicy status

#### 2.7 GitHub Actions Workflow Pattern

Four workflows, all using reusable workflows from `konecta-ix-services/.github-private`:
1. `validate-and-plan.yml` - Auto on PR, detects changed TF directories, validates + plans
2. `validate-plan-apply.yml` - Manual dispatch, env selection, branch protection, approval gate
3. `argocd-deploy.yml` - Manual dispatch for ArgoCD Application deployment
4. `manual-validate-plan.yml` - Manual dispatch for validate + plan only

All use `static-ip-runner`, Terraform version `1.7.4`, Trivy with `MEDIUM` severity.

### 3. Database and Data Layer

#### 3.1 Key Difference: Password Auth vs IAM Auth

**Backstage** uses Cloud SQL IAM authentication (no password):
- `google_sql_user` with `type = "CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT"` (file: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf`, lines 139-144)
- Database user name: `trimsuffix(google_service_account.backstage.email, ".gserviceaccount.com")`

**IQ Portal** uses traditional password-based authentication:
- From `/tmp/kix-apps-konectaiq/backend/config/constants.ts` (lines 109-117): `DB_HOST`, `DB_PORT`, `DB_NAME`, `DB_USER`, `DB_PASSWORD`
- From `/tmp/kix-apps-konectaiq/docker-entrypoint.sh` (lines 20-59): validates DB_HOST, DB_NAME, DB_USER, DB_PASSWORD at startup
- The Terraform must create a BUILT_IN user with a random password stored in Secret Manager

#### 3.2 Cloud SQL Database Module Interface

From `/tmp/kix-platform-modules/terraform/gcp/data-services/cloud-sql-db/variables.tf` (lines 1-115):

variable "project_id" { ... }
variable "instance_name" { ... }
variable "databases" {
  type = list(object({
    name      = string
    charset   = optional(string, "UTF8")
    collation = optional(string, "en_US.UTF8")
  }))
}
variable "users" {
  type = list(object({
    name              = string
    type              = optional(string, "BUILT_IN")
    password          = optional(string, null)
    generate_password = optional(bool, true)
    password_length   = optional(number, 32)
    password_special  = optional(bool, false)
    host              = optional(string, null)
  }))
}
```

### 5. Reference Implementations

#### 5.1 Terraform -- backend.tf

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/backend.tf` (lines 1-10)
```hcl
terraform {
  backend "gcs" {
    bucket = "kd-tfstate-services-dev"
    prefix = "services/backstage/resources"
  }
}
```
For IQ Portal: bucket = `kd-tfstate-services-{env}`, prefix = `services/kix-iqportal/resources`

#### 5.2 Terraform -- versions.tf

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/versions.tf` (lines 1-20)
```hcl
terraform {
  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
  required_providers {
    google = { source = "hashicorp/google", version = "~> 5.0" }
    kubernetes = { source = "hashicorp/kubernetes", version = "~> 2.23" }
    random = { source = "hashicorp/random", version = "~> 3.5" }
  }
}
```

#### 5.3 Terraform -- ExternalSecret Pattern

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf` (lines 148-223)

The ExternalSecret pattern uses `kubernetes_manifest` resources with:
- `apiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1`
- `kind: ExternalSecret`
- `secretStoreRef.kind: ClusterSecretStore`, `name: gcp-secret-manager`
- `target.creationPolicy: Owner`
- `data[].remoteRef.key: <secret-id-in-GCP-SM>`

For IQ Portal, each of the ~12 secret values needs its own ExternalSecret data entry, or they can be grouped logically into fewer ExternalSecret resources.

#### 5.4 Helm -- Deployment with Cloud SQL Proxy Sidecar

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/templates/deployment.yaml` (lines 98-117)

The Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar pattern:
```yaml
- name: cloud-sql-proxy
  image: {{ .Values.database.cloudSqlProxy.image }}
  args:
    - "--structured-logs"
    - "--auto-iam-authn"    # Backstage uses IAM auth; IQ Portal should NOT use this flag
    - "--port=5432"
    - "--private-ip"
    - {{ .Values.database.cloudSqlProxy.instanceConnectionName }}
```

**CRITICAL DIFFERENCE:** For IQ Portal, the `--auto-iam-authn` flag must be REMOVED because IQ Portal uses password-based auth, not IAM auth. The proxy only provides connectivity; authentication is via DB_USER/DB_PASSWORD.

#### 5.5 Helm -- Network Policy Pattern

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/helm/backstage/templates/networkpolicy.yaml` (274 lines)

Contains 10 network policies in a single file:
1. Default deny all ingress
2. Allow GCP health check IPs (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
3. Allow from kube-system
4. Allow GMP collectors (optional)
5. Custom ingress rules
6. Allow DNS egress to kube-system
7. Allow Google APIs egress (199.36.153.8/30, 199.36.153.4/30)
8. Allow internet egress (0.0.0.0/0 minus private ranges)
9. Custom egress rules
10. Allow same-namespace communication

#### 5.6 Helm -- GKE Gateway + IAP + Cloud Armor

Three files work together:
- `gateway.yaml` - Gateway with certmap annotation, HTTP + HTTPS listeners
- `gcpbackendpolicy.yaml` - IAP config (clientID + oauthSecretName), Cloud Armor policy, logging, timeoutSec
- `healthcheckpolicy.yaml` - Health check configuration targeting the service

#### 5.7 Script -- generate-helm-values.sh

File: `/tmp/kix-services-backstage/scripts/generate-helm-values.sh` (297 lines)

This script reads `config.json` with `jq` and generates `values-{env}.yaml`. Key features:
- Supports `--check` mode for CI validation
- Generates: image, serviceAccount, database, backstage.config, secrets, gateway, iap, securityPolicy, networkPolicies, commonLabels
- Uses heredoc pattern for YAML generation
- Handles optional blocks (auth.guest, GitHub integrations)

For IQ Portal, this script needs significant adaptation because:
- IQ Portal has no backstage-specific config (app/backend URLs, catalog)
- IQ Portal has more env vars (DB_PASSWORD, JWT_SECRET, GCS buckets, Vertex AI, ChannelHub)
- IQ Portal deployment uses different env var injection pattern

### 6. Validation Strategy

#### 6.1 Available Validation Commands

```bash
# Terraform validation (per environment)
cd environments/{env}/terraform && terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate

# Terraform formatting
terraform fmt -check -recursive environments/

# Helm lint (per environment)
helm lint environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/ \
  -f environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values.yaml \
  -f environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values-{env}.yaml

# Helm template rendering test
helm template test environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/ \
  -f environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values.yaml \
  -f environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values-{env}.yaml

# YAML validation for ArgoCD
python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml'))"

# Generate-helm-values check mode
./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh {env} --check

# Trivy IaC scan
trivy config --config trivy.yaml environments/{env}/terraform/
```

#### 6.2 Testing Frameworks

- GitHub Actions reusable workflows: `terraform-validate.yml`, `terraform-plan.yml`, `terraform-apply.yml`, `argocd-deploy.yml` from `konecta-ix-services/.github-private`
- Trivy for IaC scanning (severity: MEDIUM)
- Terraform version: 1.7.4
- Runner: `static-ip-runner`

#### 6.3 Compatibility Requirements

- Terraform >= 1.5.0 (workflows use 1.7.4)
- Google provider ~> 5.0
- Kubernetes provider ~> 2.23
- Helm v3 chart API (apiVersion: v2)
- GKE Gateway API v1 (`gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1`)
- External Secrets Operator `external-secrets.io/v1beta1`
- ArgoCD `argoproj.io/v1alpha1`
- GKE networking API `networking.gke.io/v1`

### 7. Development Guidelines

#### 7.1 Critical Conventions to Follow

1. **Config.json is the single source of truth.** All environment-specific values derive from it. Never hardcode environment values in Terraform or Helm values files.

2. **Values-{env}.yaml are auto-generated.** Never edit manually. Edit config.json and regenerate with `./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh`.

3. **Helm templates are duplicated per environment.** This is the accepted trade-off for organizational consistency per the brainstorming decision document.

4. **Terraform creates GCP resources; Helm manages K8s workloads.** The boundary is clear: Terraform handles namespace, service accounts, IAM, secrets, database, certificates. Helm handles deployment, service, HPA, PDB, network policies, gateway/routes.

5. **ExternalSecrets bridge the gap.** Terraform creates GCP Secret Manager secrets and ExternalSecret CRDs. ESO syncs them to K8s Secrets. Helm references K8s Secrets in deployment env vars.

6. **No service account keys.** All authentication uses Workload Identity.

7. **EU data residency only.** Secret replication to `europe-west1` and `europe-west4` only.

#### 7.2 Key Differences Between Backstage and IQ Portal

| Aspect | Backstage | IQ Portal |
|--------|-----------|-----------|
| DB Auth | IAM (Cloud SQL IAM SA) | Password (BUILT_IN user) |
| DB User creation | `google_sql_user` type `CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` | `google_sql_user` type `BUILT_IN` with random password |
| Cloud SQL Proxy flags | `--auto-iam-authn` | NO `--auto-iam-authn` |
| Secret count | ~4 (IAP, backend, GitHub x4) | ~12+ (DB creds, JWT, Google OAuth, GCS x3, ChannelHub x3, Vertex AI, RAG) |
| App port | 7007 | 8080 |
| Health check path | `/healthcheck` | `/health` |
| Ingress | Gateway API only | Gateway API (frontend) + Kong (API) |
| App config | ConfigMap with Backstage YAML | Environment variables from Secrets |
| ServiceAccount project | `kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-data` | `kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke` |
| Additional IAM roles | cloudsql.client, cloudsql.instanceUser | + storage.objectAdmin (GCS), aiplatform.user (Vertex AI), secretmanager.secretAccessor |

#### 7.3 GCP Configuration per Environment

From brainstorming doc (lines 286-304):

| Resource | Dev | QA | Prod |
|----------|-----|-----|------|
| GKE Project | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke` |
| Data Project | `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data` |
| AI Project | `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-ai` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-ai` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-ai` |
| Security Project | `kd-ix-eur-dev-security` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-security` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-security` |
| GKE Cluster | `kd-ix-eur-dev-cluster` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-cluster` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-cluster` |
| Cloud SQL Instance | `kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform` | `kd-ix-eur-qa-cloudsql-platform` | `kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform` |
| TF State Bucket | `kd-tfstate-services-dev` | `kd-tfstate-services-qa` | `kd-tfstate-services-prod` |

Network CIDRs per environment (from Backstage config.json cross-reference):
- **Dev:** service_cidr=`10.20.0.0/20`, master_cidr=`172.16.0.0/28`, cloudsql_psa_cidr=`10.4.0.0/16`
- **QA:** service_cidr=`10.36.0.0/20`, master_cidr=`172.16.4.0/28`, cloudsql_psa_cidr=`10.12.0.0/16`

#### 7.4 Platform Module Usage

The Backstage reference repo uses **inline resources** rather than platform modules for its Terraform. However, the platform modules are available for use:

- **Workload Identity** (`/tmp/kix-platform-modules/terraform/gcp/workload-identity/`): Creates GCP SA + WI binding + cross-project IAM roles in one module call. Source ref: `github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/workload-identity?ref=v1.0.0`

- **Secret Manager** (`/tmp/kix-platform-modules/terraform/gcp/secret-manager/`): Creates secret + optional random value + IAM bindings. Source ref: `github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=v1.0.0`

- **Cloud SQL DB** (`/tmp/kix-platform-modules/terraform/gcp/data-services/cloud-sql-db/`): Creates databases + users on existing instance with security validation. Source ref: `github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/cloud-sql-db?ref=v1.0.0`

The decision of whether to use modules or inline resources should match the Backstage pattern (inline) for consistency, unless there is a strong reason to prefer modules.

#### 7.5 Target Repository Structure

From brainstorming doc (lines 308-367):

```
kix-workload-konectaiq/
  .github/workflows/

./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh qa --check\n  RUN ./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh prod --check\n```\n\n### Key Differences from Reference (Backstage → IQ Portal)\n\n```yaml\nDATABASE_AUTH:\n  backstage: \"IAM auth (google_sql_user type CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT)\"\n  iqportal: \"Password auth (google_sql_user type BUILT_IN with random_password)\"\n  impact: \"Remove --auto-iam-authn from proxy, add DB_PASSWORD to ExternalSecret\"\n\nAPP_PORT:\n  backstage: \"7007\"\n  iqportal: \"8080\"\n  impact: \"Change containerPort, service targetPort, health check port\"\n\nHEALTH_CHECK:\n  backstage: \"/healthcheck\"\n  iqportal: \"/health (liveness), /api/health (readiness)\"\n  impact: \"Different probe paths, different health check policy path\"\n\nSECRETS:\n  backstage: \"~4 (IAP oauth, backend secret, GitHub app x4)\"\n  iqportal: \"~12 (DB creds, JWT, Google OAuth, GCS x3, ChannelHub x3, RAG token)\"\n  impact: \"More ExternalSecret data entries, more Secret Manager resources in TF\"\n\nCONFIGMAP:\n  backstage: \"Yes (app-config.yaml rendered from values)\"\n  iqportal: \"No (all config via env vars)\"\n  impact: \"Remove configmap.yaml template, remove configmap volume mount from deployment\"\n\nIAM_ROLES:\n  backstage: \"cloudsql.client, cloudsql.instanceUser\"\n  iqportal: \"cloudsql.client + storage.objectAdmin (data project) + aiplatform.user (AI project) + cloudtasks.enqueuer (data project) + secretmanager.secretAccessor (data project)\"\n  impact: \"Additional cross-project IAM binding resources in Terraform\"\n\nSERVICE_ACCOUNT_PROJECT:\n  backstage: \"kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-data\"\n  iqportal: \"kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke\"\n  impact: \"Different project reference in SA creation and WI binding\"\n\nCONTAINER_COMMAND:\n  backstage: \"node packages/backend --config /app/app-config.yaml\"\n  iqportal: \"None (uses Dockerfile ENTRYPOINT: docker-entrypoint.sh → npm run server)\"\n  impact: \"No command/args in deployment spec\"\n\nINGRESS:\n  backstage: \"Gateway API only\"\n  iqportal: \"Gateway API (frontend) — Kong managed separately via platform\"\n  impact: \"Same Gateway templates as Backstage for now; Kong route is platform-managed\"\n```\n\n### Integration Points\n\n```yaml\nTERRAFORM_STATE:\n  backend: \"GCS bucket kd-tfstate-services-{env}\"\n  prefix: \"services/kix-iqportal/resources\"\n\nGCP_SECRET_MANAGER:\n  secrets_created_by_terraform:\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-db-password\"        # Auto-generated random password\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-jwt-secret\"         # Auto-generated random secret\n  secrets_referenced_not_created:\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-google-client-id\"        # Manually populated\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-rag-pipeline-token\"      # Manually populated\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-channelhub-client-id\"    # Manually populated\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-channelhub-client-secret\" # Manually populated\n    - \"iqportal-{env}-channelhub-connection-id\" # Manually populated\n\nEXTERNAL_SECRETS_OPERATOR:\n  cluster_secret_store: \"gcp-secret-manager\"  # Pre-provisioned by platform\n  external_secrets_created_by_terraform:\n    - \"kix-app-iqportal-db-credentials\"     # DB_USER + DB_PASSWORD\n    - \"kix-app-iqportal-app-secrets\"        # JWT_SECRET, GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID, etc.\n    - \"kix-app-iqportal-integration-secrets\" # ChannelHub, RAG token\n    - \"kix-app-iqportal-storage-config\"     # GCS bucket names\n    - \"kix-app-iqportal-iap-oauth\"          # IAP OAuth secret (for GCPBackendPolicy)\n\nARGOCD:\n  project: \"platform\"  # Or \"default\" — verify from cluster\n  source: \"https://github.com/konecta-ix-workloads/kix-workload-konectaiq\"\n  path: \"environments/{env}/helm/kix-app-iqportal\"\n  value_files: [\"values.yaml\", \"values-{env}.yaml\"]\n\nGITHUB_ACTIONS:\n  reusable_workflows_org: \"konecta-ix-services/.github-private\"\n  required_secrets: [\"GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER\", \"GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT\"]\n  runner: \"static-ip-runner\"\n  terraform_version: \"1.7.4\"\n```\n\n## Validation Loop\n\n### Level 1: Syntax & Style\n\n```bash\n# Terraform validation (run per environment)\nfor env in dev qa prod; do\n  terraform -chdir=environments/$env/terraform init -backend=false\n  terraform -chdir=environments/$env/terraform validate\ndone\n\n# Terraform formatting\nterraform fmt -check -recursive environments/\n\n# Helm linting (run per environment)\nfor env in dev qa prod; do\n  helm lint environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/ \\\n    -f environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values.yaml \\\n    -f environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values-$env.yaml\ndone\n\n# Helm template rendering\nfor env in dev qa prod; do\n  helm template test environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/ \\\n    -f environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values.yaml \\\n    -f environments/$env/helm/kix-app-iqportal/values-$env.yaml > /dev/null\ndone\n\n# Values generation check\nfor env in dev qa prod; do\n  ./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh $env --check\ndone\n\n# YAML validation for ArgoCD\nfor env in dev qa prod; do\n  python3 -c \"import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('environments/$env/argocd/application.yaml'))\"\ndone\n```\n\n## Final Validation Checklist\n\n- [ ] Terraform validates: `terraform validate` for all 3 environments\n- [ ] Terraform formatted: `terraform fmt -check -recursive`\n- [ ] Helm lints: `helm lint` for all 3 environments\n- [ ] Helm templates: `helm template` renders for all 3 environments\n- [ ] Values generated: `generate-helm-values.sh --check` for all 3 environments\n- [ ] ArgoCD YAML valid: Python yaml.safe_load for all 3 environments\n- [ ] GitHub Actions YAML valid: No syntax errors\n- [ ] No hardcoded secrets in any files\n- [ ] All PLACEHOLDER values clearly marked\n- [ ] config.json per environment has consistent schema\n\n---\n\n## Anti-Patterns to Avoid\n\n- Do NOT manually edit `values-{env}.yaml` — always regenerate from config.json\n- Do NOT use `--auto-iam-authn` on Cloud SQL proxy — IQ Portal uses password auth\n- Do NOT create service account keys — Workload Identity only\n- Do NOT hardcode GCP project IDs in Terraform — always reference from config.json via locals\n- Do NOT create new Cloud SQL instances — use existing shared instances\n- Do NOT deviate from the Backstage directory structure pattern\n- Do NOT use public container images — use approved images from Artifact Registry\n- Do NOT put secrets in config.json — secret IDs only, not values\n\n---\n\n## Task Breakdown Reference\n\nSee: `docs/tasks/iqportal-workload-infrastructure.md`\n"
}

## PRP Analysis Summary

- **Feature**: IQ Portal Workload Infrastructure -- complete IaC repository for deploying KonectaIQ Portal to GKE across dev, qa, and prod
- **Key Technical Requirements**: Password-based DB auth (not IAM), cross-project IAM for 5 GCP projects, ~12 ExternalSecrets, GKE Gateway with IAP, Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar without `--auto-iam-authn`
- **Validation Requirements**: terraform validate/fmt, helm lint/template, generate-helm-values.sh --check, YAML validation for ArgoCD and workflows

## Task Complexity Assessment

- **Overall Rating**: Complex (9/10 PRP confidence, ~85 files, multi-environment)
- **Integration Points**: 5 GCP projects per environment, GKE Gateway API, ArgoCD, ESO, Cloud SQL, Artifact Registry, GitHub Actions reusable workflows
- **Primary Technical Challenge**: The Backstage-to-IQ Portal adaptation requires careful divergence in DB auth model, secret management (4 to 12 secrets), deployment template (env vars instead of configmap), and IAM scope (2 to 5 cross-project bindings)

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/kix-workload-konectaiq/README.md",
  "content": "# kix-workload-konectaiq\n\nWorkload infrastructure repository for the KonectaIQ Portal application. Contains all Terraform, Helm, ArgoCD, and CI/CD configuration required to deploy the IQ Portal to the kix-infrastructure GKE platform.\n\n## Repository Structure\n\n```\nenvironments/\n  {env}/                        # dev, qa, prod\n    config.json                 # Single source of truth for environment\n    argocd/application.yaml     # ArgoCD Application manifest\n    helm/kix-app-iqportal/      # Helm chart\n      Chart.yaml\n      values.yaml               # Base defaults\n      values-{env}.yaml         # AUTO-GENERATED from config.json\n      templates/                # Kubernetes resource templates\n    terraform/                  # GCP + K8s infrastructure\n      main.tf, variables.tf, outputs.tf, versions.tf, backend.tf, locals.tf\nscripts/\n  generate-helm-values.sh       # Generates values-{env}.yaml from config.json\n.github/workflows/              # CI/CD pipelines\n```\n\n## Key Conventions\n\n- **`config.json`** is the single source of truth per environment. All environment-specific values derive from it.\n- **`values-{env}.yaml`** files are auto-generated. Never edit manually. Edit `config.json` then run:\n  ```bash\n  ./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh <env>\n  ```\n- **Helm templates** are duplicated per environment directory (intentional for consistency).\n- **Terraform** creates GCP resources (namespace, IAM, secrets, database, certificates). **Helm** manages K8s workloads.\n\n## Workflows\n\n| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| `validate-and-plan` | PR to main | Auto Terraform validate + plan |\n| `validate-plan-apply` | Manual | Terraform validate + plan + apply with approval |\n| `argocd-deploy` | Manual | Deploy ArgoCD Application |\n| `manual-validate-plan` | Manual | Terraform validate + plan only |\n\n## Related Repositories\n\n- **Application code**: [kix-apps-konectaiq](https://github.com/konecta-ix-applications/kix-apps-konectaiq)\n- **Platform docs**: [kix-platform-docs](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs)\n- **Platform modules**: [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)\n"
}
```

     7→```
     8→environments/

    10→    config.json                 # Single source of truth for environment
    11→    argocd/application.yaml     # ArgoCD Application manifest

    23→
    24→## Key Conventions
    25→
    26→- **`config.json`** is the single source of truth per environment. All environment-specific values derive from it.
    27→- **`values-{env}.yaml`** files are auto-generated. Never edit manually. Edit `config.json` then run:
    28→  ```bash
    29→  ./scripts/generate-helm-values.sh <env>
    30→  ```
    31→- **Helm templates** are duplicated per environment directory (intentional for consistency).
    32→- **Terraform** creates GCP resources (namespace, IAM, secrets, database, certificates). **Helm** manages K8s workloads.
    33→
    34→## Workflows
    35→
    36→| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |
    37→|----------|---------|---------|
    38→| `validate-and-plan` | PR to main | Auto Terraform validate + plan |
    39→| `validate-plan-apply` | Manual | Terraform validate + plan + apply with approval |
    40→| `argocd-deploy` | Manual | Deploy ArgoCD Application |
    41→| `manual-validate-plan` | Manual | Terraform validate + plan only |

{
  "description": "Create Terraform files for all envs",
  "subagent_type": "terraform-infrastructure-expert",
  "prompt": "Create all Terraform files for the IQ Portal workload repository across 3 environments (dev, qa, prod).\n\nIMPORTANT: Read the reference Terraform files from /tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/ FIRST to understand the exact patterns. Then adapt for IQ Portal.\n\nThe key differences from Backstage:\n1. **Password-based DB auth** (not IAM) - use google_sql_user type \"BUILT_IN\" with random_password\n2. **More IAM roles** - need storage.objectAdmin, aiplatform.user, cloudtasks.enqueuer in addition to cloudsql.client\n3. **More secrets** (~12 vs ~4) - DB password, JWT secret, Google OAuth, RAG token, ChannelHub creds, GCS buckets\n4. **Service account in GKE project** - not data project\n5. **Cross-project IAM** - SA in kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke needs access to resources in kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-data and kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-ai\n\n## Files to create (same for all 3 environments):\n\n### versions.tf (identical across all envs)\nPath: /home/nathan_booth/code/kix-workload-konectaiq/environments/{env}/terraform/versions.tf\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.5.0\"\n  required_providers {\n    google = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/google\"\n      version = \"~> 5.0\"\n    }\n    kubernetes = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/kubernetes\"\n      version = \"~> 2.23\"\n    }\n    random = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/random\"\n      version = \"~> 3.5\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### backend.tf (env-specific bucket)\n- dev: bucket = \"kd-tfstate-services-dev\", prefix = \"services/kix-iqportal/resources\"\n- qa: bucket = \"kd-tfstate-services-qa\", prefix = \"services/kix-iqportal/resources\"\n- prod: bucket = \"kd-tfstate-services-prod\", prefix = \"services/kix-iqportal/resources\"\n\n### locals.tf (identical across all envs)\nLoads config.json:\n```hcl\nlocals {\n  config  = jsondecode(file(\"${path.module}/../config.json\"))\n  global  = local.config.global\n  service = local.config.service\n}\n```\n\n### variables.tf\nInclude variables for database init job trigger (matching Backstage pattern).\n\n### main.tf\nThis is the largest file. MIRROR the structure from /tmp/kix-services-backstage/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf but adapt for IQ Portal:\n\n1. **Providers** - google + kubernetes (same pattern as Backstage)\n2. **Data sources** - google_client_config, google_container_cluster\n3. **Kubernetes namespace** - local.service.namespace with labels\n4. **GCP Service Account** - in local.service.gcp_service_account.project (the GKE project)\n5. **IAM roles on GKE project** - cloudsql.client\n6. **Cross-project IAM roles on data project** (kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-data):\n   - roles/storage.objectAdmin\n   - roles/cloudtasks.enqueuer\n   - roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor\n7. **Cross-project IAM roles on AI project** (kd-ix-eur-{env}-platform-ai):\n   - roles/aiplatform.user\n8. **Kubernetes Service Account** - with iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account annotation\n9. **Workload Identity binding** - google_service_account_iam_binding\n10. **Random passwords** - db_password (32 chars), jwt_secret (64 chars)\n11. **GCP Secret Manager secrets** - Create secrets and versions for auto-generated values (db_password, jwt_secret)\n12. **Cloud SQL database** - google_sql_database on existing instance\n13. **Cloud SQL user** - google_sql_user type BUILT_IN with random password\n14. **ExternalSecrets** - kubernetes_manifest resources:\n    - kix-app-iqportal-db-credentials (DB_USER, DB_PASSWORD from Secret Manager)\n    - kix-app-iqportal-app-secrets (JWT_SECRET, GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID from Secret Manager)\n    - kix-app-iqportal-integration-secrets (CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID, CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET, CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID, RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN)\n    - kix-app-iqportal-storage-config (GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET, GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET, GCS_RAG_BUCKET)\n    - kix-app-iqportal-iap-oauth (IAP OAuth secret for GCPBackendPolicy)\n15. **Database init job** (optional, matching Backstage pattern) - Kubernetes job to grant privileges\n16. **Certificate Manager** - google_certificate_manager_certificate, certificate_map, certificate_map_entry\n\nFor ExternalSecrets, use this pattern from Backstage:\n```hcl\nresource \"kubernetes_manifest\" \"external_secret_name\" {\n  manifest = {\n    apiVersion = \"external-secrets.io/v1beta1\"\n    kind       = \"ExternalSecret\"\n    metadata = {\n      name      = \"secret-name\"\n      namespace = local.service.namespace\n    }\n    spec = {\n      refreshInterval = \"1h\"\n      secretStoreRef = {\n        kind = \"ClusterSecretStore\"\n        name = \"gcp-secret-manager\"\n      }\n      target = {\n        name           = \"secret-name\"\n        creationPolicy = \"Owner\"\n      }\n      data = [\n        {\n          secretKey = \"KEY_NAME\"\n          remoteRef = {\n            key = local.service.secrets.secret_id\n          }\n        }\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### outputs.tf\nExport key resource identifiers: namespace, SA email, WI binding, ExternalSecret names, database details, certificate info.\n\nIMPORTANT:\n- Create parent directories as needed\n- Make sure all 3 environments have identical Terraform code EXCEPT backend.tf (different bucket)\n- Reference all values from config.json via locals (local.global.*, local.service.*)\n- Do NOT hardcode any environment-specific values in main.tf\n- Run terraform fmt formatting on all files",
  "run_in_background": true
}
